These trees define the world we're painting. voxsim already has beautiful trees (crates/vox-trees in ~/work/voxsim; its contact sheets are in ~/work/voxsim/docs/paint-baseline/trees/, and David called them perfect). Your aim is to see how far the student approach can go past them on these four species: the drooping leader and weeping sprays of hemlock, the buttressed fluted base and fanned sprays of cedar, the deep-furrowed bark and layered crown of old Douglas-fir, and the stiff blue-green spruce of the rainforest.

# What earlier students discovered: notes on their craft

These are notes on the painstaking work of the students who came before you:

- four who each spent hours learning Ferrari's language on one small scene (`pedagogy/{atelier,master-copy,analysis,observation}`);
- seven who studied single topics.

They copied his pixels crop by crop, measured his grass against real grass, built a boulder up from a lit sphere, and diagrammed his picture before daring to paint it. What they found is here to inspire you and save you time. It isn't a specification. You'll see things they didn't, and some of what they wrote will turn out wrong or incomplete for your subject. Follow your own eyes. Their best work is in their intermediate studies (e.g. `master-copy/out/cmp_c*.png`, `atelier/stage2/`, `observation/studies/translate_*.png`, `analysis/img/`); those are worth looking at more than their final painters.

The tags below say how each finding came about:

- **[shown]**: a student built it and it worked.
- **[measured]**: a student measured it in Ferrari's pixels or in photos.
- **[mine]**: the integrator's reading, not yet tried by anyone.

Striking to us: the four students, working apart, found much the same underlying ideas.

## The convergence

Working alone, all four arms landed on the same architecture:

- **Paint indices, not colours.**
  - Master copy found that Ferrari's index map is literally a form map: indices are bins of surface direction, and "the palette is the light". The same index image serves morning and afternoon. [measured]
  - Atelier paints in "step space" along one material ramp. [shown]
  - Sky found that two of Ferrari's hour pairs are identical pixels. [measured]
  - Analysis found that value is the structure and colour is temperature and distance. [measured]
- **Two light families that never touch.** Atelier's terminator rule [shown], analysis's three-value picture plan with every mass kept inside its group [measured], and rock's lit and shadow groups [shown] are the same idea at two scales.
- **Dither mixes only adjacent steps of one ramp.** All four arms use it. Ordered 2×2 dither suits hard or precise things (rock, sky seams, water rows); random or diffusion dither suits organic texture (lawns, foam). Master copy measured this [measured]; analysis cites Ferrari's GDC talk for the same split.
- **The medium's own geometry.** Contours are clean runs (1:1, 2:1, 4-4-4 flats), measured on Ferrari's crests by analysis and atelier [measured]. Atelier redraws every major contour by snapping edges to clean ratios [shown]. Masses meet level ground or water in a dead-flat run with a 1-px darker contact line [measured: analysis, observation].
- **Clean runs are for narrow forms; mass boundaries are where texture ends.** David's correction (2026-09-25): the clean-run rule came from the *grass strokes*. It applies to anything narrow, drawn as a line: blades, cracks, joints, twigs, conifer spires, stems, a snowline drawn as a stroke. A 1–2 px fo

# Willow scrub, after Mark Ferrari: lessons

A student's log. Subject: the low many-stemmed shrubby willows of braided rivers and gravel bars.
Code in this folder: `wpaint.py` (index canvas, clean runs, sheets), `bush.py` (form: wands, lobes,
value plan), `foliage.py` (the marks: clump grammar, speckle, leaf glyphs, fringe, wand tips),
`willow.py` (the painter), `lw.py` + `copyharness.py` + `stage0_copy.py` (master copies),
`transfer.py` (copy -> willow steps), one script per later stage.

Photos: `refs/` (fetched with the studio's imgfetch; `refs/contact.jpg` is the contact sheet). The ones
I lean on: `crop_nzbush.png` (a single low willow on tussock, NZ), the Rio Grande band
(`refs/more/openverse_Rio_Grande_in_White_Rock_Canyon_*`), the Alaskan gold willow flats
(`refs/more/openverse_Be_Careful_*`), Salix triandra on a river bank, and the two in `transfer/refs`.

---

## Stage 0: copy Ferrari first, so the technique has a proven target

Ferrari never painted willows, so I copied the passages of his closest to them: leafy masses in
*Living Worlds* "Deep Forest" (V09), read from the original index maps (`lw.py`).

- **C1**, the sunlit foreground bush, native (0,322)-(140,380): near-scale leaves.
- **C2**, the middle-distance hedge band behind the pool, native (265,222)-(395,282): the closest
  thing in his work to a band of scrub along water, with its crest against the sky and stems in
  the dark below.

The copy uses his palette and only the copyist's sketch (which ramp owns each region, and a blurred
light field, as the master-copy study did). Everything at pixel level is generated.

### What reading his indices showed

- **Ramps are per plant, stacked vertically inside a leaf.** In C1 there are three little ramps
  (lime, olive, dim green) that share one near-black. Down a column, a lit pixel sits on the next
  step of its own ramp (lime 9 is above green 10 39% of the time), then darker, then black: every
  leaf is lit on its upper edge and darkens downward.
- **Lit components are tiny.** 46% of the brightest-step components are single pixels, 9% vertical
  pairs, 9% horizontal pairs. A leaf is a composite of a lit top row (1–4 px) over a mid row, with
  1-px stems (vertical runs of the mid step, 3–6 px) among them.
- **The middle distance is bimodal.** In C2 the base is two close darks (L 59/79) and the marks two
  lights (L 131/175); the middle step (L 103) is only 8% of pixels. Contrast lives inside the mark,
  the mass stays calm.
- **The far hedge (C ramp) is lighter, bluer and flatter**, mostly the two middle steps in checker.

### The technique, as discovered (all in `foliage.py`)

1. **The clump grammar** (`clump_foliage`), which the critic pointed me to and which is the big one.
   Foliage is a heap of clumps (C1: 5–12 px; C2: 4–13 px), painted top to bottom so lower clumps lie
   in front. Each clump has its own light: a lit cap toward the sun, a body, a dark band along its
   bottom

### How the form is built (`bush.py`, `WillowBush`)

- **A fan of wands, not a heightfield blob.** 30 wands leave a crown about a third of the bush wide,
  each a cubic curve that rises, leans out and droops at the tip; the most splayed wands droop
  nearly to the gravel. Wands are grouped in 4–7 clumps (a shared tilt and azimuth), which is what
  gives the dome its sub-domes. Leaf lobes sit along the outer 90% of each wand.
- **Three scales of normal.** An egg fitted to the whole mass (the big form: top lit, underside
  turning down), a smoothed dome per wand clump, and each lobe's own sphere, mixed 0.7 / 0.35 / rest.
  The first versions used only lobe normals and self-shadow marching on the lobe heightfield: that
  gave hard black polygons and lightning-bolt shadows (critic, round 1). Blurring the lobe normals
  and dropping the cast-shadow march fixed it; the egg made it read as one bush instead of a heap
  of balls.
- **Lobe caps break the terminator.** A lobe whose own top faces the sun keeps a lit cap up to 0.35
  past the big terminator, so the family boundary follows lobes, not a straight cut.
- **The hollow under the canopy.** Near the bottom centre the clumps thin out and the mass becomes a
  dark hollow (the far side of the bush in shade) with the stems in front of it, as in the NZ photo.
  My first attempt removed the foliage there and showed sky through a bridge-shaped arch; the
  second filled whole columns and made a box. Stems from a narrow crown read as one trunk; from a
  crown a third of the bush wide, diverging early, they read as a fan.
- **Back light.** Thin leaves glow at the silhouette when the sun is behind: a rim 3–4 px deep,
  weighted by how much the edge faces the sun and broken by noise (bottom row).

### Values

The greys keep the luminance of the colour ramps (Ferrari's hedge: lit base 59/79, marks 131/175;
near darks 23–70). Measured on the painted bush: lit family mean 0.356, shadow 0.149, top-step share
in the lit family 0.15. His sunlit hedge's lit ramp averages 0.355, so the value key is his.

### The edge

The crest is the union of clump outlines (rounded caps, lit on top), plus a few lit wand tips and
1-px holes. See `stage1/s1_edge.png` (photo | mine | his, 8×) and `stage1/s1_compare.png`.
In greyscale the bush looks dimmer than it does in colour (`stage1/s1_colourcheck_ferrari_palette.png`):
his hedge does too; hue carries part of the separation between marks and base.

### Honest state

- The notans and the form field read as a willow dome under all four suns; the right-back case
  still has two thin sliver shapes where lobe caps cross.
- Painted, it reads as Ferrari-language foliage, but at this stage as a generic shrub. The willow
  specifics (narrow leaves along upright wands, silvery undersides, stems) are stage 2 and 3.
- The crest on the shadow side is dark and busy. His shadowed crests are calmer: fewer marks, the
  clump outline alone.

# vox-trees — painterly pixel-art tree sprites from tree structure

Zero runtime deps (image is a dev-dep for the sheets). Lane: `lane-trees`.

## API (what a renderer calls)



use vox_trees::*;
// 1. Structure: from flora (branch skeleton + clusters) or the fallback generator.
let t: TreeSketch = sketch_with(&amp;SketchParams { species: Archetype::Oak, height_m: 19.0, crown_w_m: 16.0,
    age: 0.6, lean: [0.0, 0.0], season: Season::Summer, damage: 0.2, seed,
    setting: Setting { openness: 0.8, light: [0.3, 0.0], wind: [0.0, 0.0], neighbors: vec![] } });
// (also sketch_from_params(species, h, w, age, lean, season, damage, seed) — the brief&#x27;s signature)
// 2. Paint.
let ctx = PaintCtx::new(meters_per_pixel, sun_dir(hour, day_of_year, lat), View::iso(rot));
let key = cache_key(&amp;t, &amp;ctx);          // sketch hash + mpp (5% steps) + view + sun bucket
let s: Sprite = paint(&amp;t, &amp;ctx);        // or paint_many(&amp;[Placed{sketch,pos}], &amp;ctx) for canopy
// 3. Colour: palette indices -&gt; RGB via a (tintable) palette.
let ramps = Ramps::tinted(Tint::from_hour(hour));   // time of day = palette swap, sprite stays cached
let rgba = s.to_rgba(&amp;ramps);




`Sprite { w, h, anchor (ground point px), px: Vec<u8> palette index (0 = clear), lift: Vec<i16>
(px above ground point, for depth compositing against terrain), shadow: ShadowMask { w, h, anchor,
px: 0 none / 1 penumbra (dither it) / 2 full } }`. The shadow is cast along the sun onto the tree's
ground plane; the renderer darkens ground under it (multiply by its shadow colour).

Palette: `Ramp` enum (45 ramps × 5 levels, `1 + ramp*5 + level`). Level 0 deep shadow (hue toward
blue-violet), 1 shadow, 2 mid, 3 light (toward yellow), 4 accent (hand-placed highlights only).
**vox-paint integration:** its Canvas takes per-image custom ramps; register our 45 ramps
(`Ramps::ramp(r)`) as custom ramps once, and write each sprite pixel as (custom_base + ramp,
tone = level/4). vox-paint's `Grade` then grades them like its own ramps. `View { yaw, elev,
z_scale }` — set `z_scale` to vox-paint's `zf` so heights match its projection.

Scales (`Scale::for_mpp`, override with `ctx.scale`): Scene < 0.17 m/px, Landscape < 1.0,
Region ≥ 1.0. Each is designed, not shrunk (see below).

## How the painting works (painter's order)

1. **Structure first.** Shape, mass distribution and character come only from the sketch:
   the crown is the union of clusters carried by the limbs. No templates.
2. **Broad light.** Every leaf bundle is lit by a blend of its cluster's ellipsoid normal and the
   whole crown's normal (Scene 35% crown, Landscape 50%: bigger, simpler masses when small), plus
   sky term, AO for interior bundles, and **cast shadow from clusters between it and the sun**
   via an opacity shadow map (density binned in slices along the sun on a grid ⊥ sun; O(1)
   queries). Wood gets the same map (trunks in the crown's shade).
3. **Texture.** Species leaf-bundle modules (Lobed oak, Spray beech, Drop birch, Pinnate
   walnut, Ne

# Exemplars

Student paintings that David singled out as having real magic (2026-09-25): "nothing has come close to the magic of our dear students." Worth sitting with before painting anything.

- `atelier_boulder_greyscale_form.png`: the atelier student's granite boulder in greyscale (pedagogy/atelier/stage4/zoom_boulder.png). A heavy, turned form in a handful of values. The light plane is broad and flecked, the shadow side is simple and dark, grass overlaps the foot, and the edge is found against the light and lost in the shadow.
- `student_boulder_dusk_tarn.png`: a boulder sunk in a dusk grass bank at a tarn (from a student's parameter sheet, later overwritten). One pale lit face against a big dark mass: the form is carried by light and palette with extreme economy. Warm ochre grass strokes, a mirrored dark reflection, and a violet evening key.

What they share: big simple masses, few values, the light doing the work, deliberate marks, and patience.

"""Load Ferrari's Living Worlds scenes as index maps (format from the Canvas Cycle demo)."""
import re, json, numpy as np
REF = "~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/master-copy/ref/lw"
def load(name):
    t = open(f"{REF}/{name}.json").read()
    t = re.sub(r'([{,])\s*([A-Za-z_]+)\s*:', r'\1"\2":', t).replace("'", '"')
    d = json.loads(t)
    d["pal"] = np.array(d["colors"], dtype=np.uint8)
    d["idx"] = np.array(d["pixels"], dtype=np.int32).reshape(d["height"], d["width"])
    return d
def lum(rgb):
    rgb = np.asarray(rgb, float)
    return 0.2126 * rgb[..., 0] + 0.7152 * rgb[..., 1] + 0.0722 * rgb[..., 2]

# output:
55-**The rock mass** is dark violet, broken by an irregular checker of mid violet in patches. It is not a uniform checker; my first version was, and it looked mechanical. Lit colour goes almost only on the crest rim facing the sun, 1–2 px of hot orange. Just inside the rim there is a broken **echo** line 2–3 px lower, parallel to the crest: a stacked lit ledge. The shadow-side crest keeps a 1-px mid outline so the edge reads against the sky. Snow in shade is the sky's own lilac and sun-struck snow is the sky's own peach, so the palette is shared between materials. The highest rock catches the most light (alpenglow).
56-
57:**The pines** are a 1-px spire for about 4 rows, then crosses on every other row. Tiers of long branches come every 4–5 rows, the half-width is about 1 + 0.15·d, and roughly 15–20% of the canopy is holes. Lit ochre sits on the ends of branches on the sun side, sparse in the interior.
58-
59-**Craft**, added after the critic's note: a ridge is not a noisy curve. It is short straight runs with regular 1:1, 2:1 and 1:2 steps. `fl.clean_curve()` snaps every segment of a copyist's polyline to a clean slope.
60-
61-Final: blurLab 6.6, hist 0.44. **Remaining gap:** Ferrari's rock has fall-line gullies with lit edges that I only approximate, and his pines carry more ochre and fuller tiers than mine.
62-
63-### Copy 2: the lit grass mound (the island) (`src/c2_mound.py`, crop (255,255,160,55))
64-![c2](out/cmp_c2.png)
65-
66-Read straight off the index map, the mound is **four form classes in a fixed spatial order: reflected light | core shadow | halftone | lit** (dusk colours (60,63,45) | (28,31,20) | (106,80,40) | (196,134,76)). The reflected light on the far edge is *lighter* than the core shadow beside it, which is the classical painter's order.
67-
68-- **Width of the transitions.** The class coordinate drifts almost linearly across the mound, about 40 px per class. The transitions are as wide as the classes, so no zone is pure: the largest single-class share anywhere is about 0.75, and three classes can mix in one place. That is modelled as `fl.scatter()`: class = round(u + 0.5·N).
69-- **The dither is not plain white noise.** Measured neighbour-difference rates: shadow-side mixes are about 0.6 against 0.5 for random, so they are checker-biased. Lit-side mixes are about 0.4, so they are clumped, because sunlit tufts catch light in small groups. This is `fl.threshold_field(checker, grain)`.

# output:
# Naturalists' studio: Washington State as our reference world

Five researchers, working as a community, study the real Washington State (the Olympic Peninsula, the Puget lowlands and the Cascades). They explain it to the people building **voxsim**, a simulated world of geology, water, plants, animals and people, painted in pixel art.

- geo/: geology and geomorphology
- hydro/: hydrology and rivers
- plants/: plant ecology and vegetation
- animals/: animal ecology
- people/: human ecology and lifeways

## Scope: all of Washington State's landscapes
Not just the Olympics and Puget lowlands. Cover the whole range, each with its own geology, water, plants, animals and people:

- the outer Pacific coast;
- the temperate rainforest of the western Olympics (Hoh, Queets, Quinault);
- the alpine zones of the Olympics and Cascades;
- meadows of every kind (subalpine, wet, dry and prairie, e.g. the South Sound prairies);
- riparian areas and the littoral of the Salish Sea (Puget Sound, Hood Canal, the San Juan Islands);
- the dry east of the Cascades, which is varied in itself: ponderosa pine foothills, shrub-steppe, the Channeled Scablands and Palouse, basalt coulees, and the Columbia and Snake rivers.

**Human ecology and the built environment target Washington before European contact:** Indigenous lifeways, settlements and structures across all these regions (coastal plank-house villages, interior pit houses and mat lodges, seasonal camps, fishing sites). Write respectfully and from scholarly and tribal sources.

## Community

- **letters/:** numbered letters between researchers, never edited once written (e.g. `007-hydro-to-geo-sediment-supply.md`). Read everyone's, answer freely, and argue when you disagree.
- **questions/:** questions from the sim and render lanes. Answer by writing `<n>-answer-<you>.md` beside the question. Anyone may answer.
- **shared/:** a shared glossary, cross-cutting guides (e.g. "what happens after a landslide", which touches all five), and the reference-area map.

## Audiences

1. **Sim lanes:** the Rust crates vox-geo, vox-hydro, vox-flora, vox-fauna and vox-human in ~/work/voxsim/crates (read their NOTES.md). They need processes, rates, thresholds, what drives what, and what matters at the sim's scales (0.5 m voxels, one sim-minute ticks, worlds a few km across).
2. **Painters:** the render and biome lanes, and the art students in ~/work/pixelart-studies. They need what things look like at each distance (close, 10 m, 100 m, 1 km, from the air), in every season and light, with pointers to good photos (~/work/pixelart-studies/tools/imgfetch.py fetches them).

## Reference data already on disk (~/work/voxsim-data, ~7 GB)

- 3DEP elevation (10 m basin-wide, 1 m lidar around the Skokomish/Lake Cushman);
- WA DNR geology (1:100k and 1:24k) and the landslide inventory;
- NHDPlus HR hydrography;
- ESA WorldCover land cover.

The notes are in ~/work/voxsim/docs/lod-scouting.md. The prototype area is UTM10 x474–490 km, y5244–5260 km (

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""imgfetch: fast, polite reference-image sourcing (Wikimedia Commons + Openverse).

  python3 imgfetch.py "cinder cone scoria slope" -n 12 -o refs/cinder
  python3 imgfetch.py -f queries.txt -n 8 -o refs            # one query per line; subdir per query
  options: --src commons,openverse   --width 1200   --sheet (writes <out>/_sheet.jpg)

Writes images plus <out>/sources.tsv (file, title, license, author, page url).
Cached: files already present are skipped. Handles 429/5xx with Retry-After backoff.
Parallel downloads (4 workers) with a shared per-host rate limiter (~3 req/s; Wikimedia's media
host is kept to 1 per 2 s and slows further on any 429, since it throttles hard per IP after bursts).
Openverse (mostly Flickr-hosted CC images) is the primary source because it stays fast.
"""
import argparse, concurrent.futures as cf, json, os, re, sys, threading, time, urllib.error, urllib.parse, urllib.request

UA = "pixelart-studies-imgfetch/1.0 (research; contact: local)"
_locks = {}; _last = {}; _glock = threading.Lock()

SLOW_HOSTS = {"upload.wikimedia.org": 2.0}   # Wikimedia media host: ~1 req/s or it 429s

def _throttle(host, min_gap=0.34):
    min_gap = SLOW_HOSTS.get(host, min_gap)
    with _glock:
        lk = _locks.setdefault(host, threading.Lock())
    with lk:
        dt = time.time() - _last.get(host, 0)
        if dt < min_gap:
            time.sleep(min_gap - dt)
        _last[host] = time.time()

def get(url, tries=4, timeout=25):
    host = urllib.parse.urlparse(url).netloc
    if host == "upload.wikimedia.org":
        tries = 2  # fail fast; other sources fill in
    for i in range(tries):
        _throttle(host)
        try:
            req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers={"User-Agent": UA})
            return urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout).read()
        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
usage: imgfetch.py [-h] [-f FILE] [-n N] [-o OUT] [--src SRC] [--width WIDTH]
                   [--sheet]
                   [query]

positional arguments:
  query

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  -f FILE, --file FILE
  -n N
  -o OUT, --out OUT
  --src SRC
  --width WIDTH
  --sheet

atelier/LESSONS.md-133-- **Mountains** are now a few massifs with straight flanks (2:1, 1:1, 3:1 slopes). Their facets fan from each summit along clean ratios, and the snowline is a triangle wave, so the couloirs are clean zigzags. My noise-based mountains before this looked like curtains, scratches and, at one point, a city skyline.

Write: elements/conifers/tree.py
"""Conifer geometry in metres: trunk, branches, and foliage points (sprays).

A tree is built the way it grows: a trunk (with lean, taper, buttress flare), branches set along
it in whorls or scattered, each branch a curve with species-specific droop/upsweep, and foliage
as *sprays* strung along the outer part of each branch. Each spray is sampled as points that
carry: position, spray normal (flat sprays face up-ish), a stroke direction (how the mark is
drawn), branch id and tier id (for clump light), and "pad" coordinates that say where in the
branch's foliage pad the point sits (top/underside/tip), which the painter turns into light.

y is up. x right, z toward the viewer.
"""
import numpy as np
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

GOLD = np.pi * (3 - np.sqrt(5))


@dataclass
class Species:
    name: str
    # crown silhouette: radius(t) for t in [0,1] from crown base to top, as r_max * f(t)
    crown_pow: float = 1.0          # 1 = cone; <1 = fuller (bullet); >1 = concave spire
    crown_round_top: float = 0.0    # 0 = pointed; 1 = flat/round top (old growth)
    crown_ratio: float = 0.6        # crown length / height (young trees override)
    rmax_frac: float = 0.18         # crown max radius / height
    # branching
    whorled: bool = True
    per_whorl: tuple = (4, 6)
    internode: float = 0.55         # metres between branch levels (young); scales with size
    base_angle: float = 0.0         # radians above horizontal at the crown middle
    top_upsweep: float = 0.6        # extra upward angle near the top
    low_droop: float = 0.3          # extra downward angle at the crown base
    droop: float = 0.35             # gravity sag (fraction of length at the tip)
    tip_upturn: float = 0.0         # J-hook upturn at the tip (cedar)
    # foliage
    spray_start: float = 0.35       # foliage from this fraction of branch length outward
    pad_width: float = 0.35         # foliage pad half-width across the branch / branch length
    pad_hang: float = 0.12          # how far foliage hangs under the branch (/ length)
    pad_top: float = 0.05           # how far above the branch (/ length)
    pendant: float = 0.0            # 0..1: sprays hang as vertical curtains (cedar/spruce branchlets)
    density: float = 1.0            # foliage point density factor
    clump: float = 0.5              # 0 = even, 1 = strongly clumped sprays along the branch
    leader_nod: float = 0.0         # drooping leader (hemlock) as fraction of top length
    stroke: str = "droop"           # mark type: droop | hang | stiff | brush
    hole_rate: float = 0.15
    bark: str = "furrow"            # furrow | fibrous | scaly | plates
    buttress: float = 0.0           # base flare strength
    trunk_frac: float = 0.012       # dbh radius / height (young)


SPECIES = {
    # Douglas-fir: pyramidal young, clear tall bole & irregular heavy crown old; brushy twigs
    "douglas": Species("douglas", crown_pow=0.95, crown_ratio=0.65, rmax_frac=0.17, whorled=True,
                       per_whorl=(4, 6), internode=0.6, base_angle=0.05, top_upsweep=0.7,
                       low_droop=0.35, droop=0.25, tip_upturn=0.08, spray_start=0.3,
                       pad_width=0.30, pad_hang=0.16, pad_top=0.07, pendant=0.25, density=1.0,
                       clump=0.6, stroke="brush", hole_rate=0.18, bark="furrow", buttress=0.15,
                       trunk_frac=0.014),
    # Western hemlock: drooping leader, slender drooping branches, lacy flat sprays
    "hemlock": Species("hemlock", crown_pow=0.85, crown_ratio=0.75, rmax_frac=0.16, whorled=False,
                       per_whorl=(1, 2), internode=0.28, base_angle=-0.05, top_upsweep=0.35,
                       low_droop=0.45, droop=0.45, tip_upturn=0.05, spray_start=0.25,
                       pad_width=0.38, pad_hang=0.10, pad_top=0.03, pendant=0.1, density=0.9,
                       clump=0.45, leader_nod=0.06, stroke="droop", hole_rate=0.2,
                       bark="scaly", buttress=0.1, trunk_frac=0.011),
    # Western redcedar: J-hook branches, hanging flat spray curtains, buttressed fluted base
    "cedar": Species("cedar", crown_pow=0.8, crown_ratio=0.75, rmax_frac=0.19, whorled=False,
                     per_whorl=(1, 2), internode=0.33, base_angle=-0.25, top_upsweep=0.8,
                     low_droop=0.4, droop=0.35, tip_upturn=0.45, spray_start=0.2,
                     pad_width=0.30, pad_hang=0.28, pad_top=0.04, pendant=0.85, density=1.0,
                     clump=0.55, leader_nod=0.03, stroke="hang", hole_rate=0.16,
                     bark="fibrous", buttress=0.6, trunk_frac=0.016),
    # Sitka spruce: stiff horizontal branches, pendulous branchlets, broad cone, blue-green
    "spruce": Species("spruce", crown_pow=1.0, crown_ratio=0.7, rmax_frac=0.2, whorled=True,
                      per_whorl=(5, 7), internode=0.5, base_angle=0.02, top_upsweep=0.55,
                      low_droop=0.2, droop=0.15, tip_upturn=0.12, spray_start=0.3,
                      pad_width=0.28, pad_hang=0.2, pad_top=0.06, pendant=0.5, density=1.1,
                      clump=0.5, stroke="stiff", hole_rate=0.14, bark="plates", buttress=0.4,
                      trunk_frac=0.015),
}


AGES = {
    # height m, crown ratio mult, internode scale, irregularity, clear-bole, snag top
    "sapling": dict(H=3.0, cr=1.0, irr=0.1, dead_low=0.0, broken=0.0, girth=1.6),
    "young": dict(H=14.0, cr=0.95, irr=0.2, dead_low=0.15, broken=0.0, girth=1.0),
    "mature": dict(H=42.0, cr=0.6, irr=0.45, dead_low=0.3, broken=0.0, girth=1.0),
    "old": dict(H=68.0, cr=0.42, irr=0.85, dead_low=0.45, broken=0.35, girth=1.5),
}


@dataclass
class TreeGeom:
    species: Species
    H: float
    trunk: np.ndarray          # (n,4) x,y,z,r along the bole
    branches: list             # list of dict(pts (m,3), r, tier, alive)
    fol: dict                  # foliage points: p (N,3), n (N,3), d (N,3) stroke dir, pad (N,3), bid, tier, tip
    crown_base: float
    crown_axis: np.ndarray     # (k,3) crown centre line
    rfun: object               # radius(y) of the crown envelope


def crown_radius(sp, t, rmax, round_top, irr_rng=None):
    """Envelope radius at crown fraction t (0 base .. 1 top)."""
    t = np.clip(t, 0, 1)
    # cone from top, rounded near base
    r = (1 - t) ** sp.crown_pow
    # base rounding: full radius a little above crown base
    r = r * np.clip(0.55 + 0.45 * np.minimum(t / 0.18, 1.0), 0, 1)
    if round_top > 0:
        # old growth: flattened, rounded top (crown stops growing up, spreads)
        rt = np.sqrt(np.clip(1 - t ** 2, 0, 1)) * 0.75
        r = (1 - round_top) * r + round_top * np.maximum(r, rt * (t < 0.97))
    return rmax * r


def build(species="douglas", age="mature", seed=0, H=None, lean=(0.0, 0.0), openness=0.6,
          density_scale=1.0, mpp=0.25, wind=(0.0, 0.0)):
    sp = SPECIES[species] if isinstance(species, str) else species
    ag = AGES[age]
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    H = H or ag["H"] * rng.uniform(0.85, 1.15)
    irr = ag["irr"]
    crown_ratio = min(0.97, sp.crown_ratio * ag["cr"] * (0.8 + 0.4 * openness))
    if age == "sapling":
        crown_ratio = 0.92
    cb = H * (1 - crown_ratio)
    rmax = sp.rmax_frac * H * (1.0 if age != "old" else 0.8) * rng.uniform(0.9, 1.1)
    if age == "sapling":
        rmax = sp.rmax_frac * H * 1.4
    round_top = {"sapling": 0, "young": 0, "mature": 0.15, "old": 0.6}[age]
    if species == "hemlock" or species == "cedar":
        round_top *= 0.6

    # ---- trunk: lean + gentle sweep; taper; buttress
    ny = 60
    ys = np.linspace(0, H, ny)
    lean = np.array(lean, float)
    sway = rng.normal(0, 1, 4)
    xs = lean[0] * ys + 0.004 * H * irr * np.sin(ys / H * np.pi * (1 + sway[0]) + sway[1])
    zs = lean[1] * ys + 0.004 * H * irr * np.sin(ys / H * np.pi * (1 + sway[2]) + sway[3])
    dbh = sp.trunk_frac * H * ag["girth"]
    rs = dbh * (1 - ys / H) ** 0.9 + 0.02
    # base flare
    flare = sp.buttress * dbh * 1.6 * np.exp(-ys / (0.05 * H + 0.6))
    rs = rs + flare
    trunk = np.stack([xs, ys, zs, rs], 1)
    # leader nod (hemlock): the top bends over
    top_len = sp.leader_nod * H
    if top_len > 0:
        k = ys > H - top_len
        u = (ys[k] - (H - top_len)) / top_len
        trunk[k, 0] += (u ** 2) * top_len * 0.7
        trunk[k, 1] -= (u ** 2.5) * top_len * 0.35

    def trunk_at(y):
        return np.array([np.interp(y, trunk[:, 1], trunk[:, 0]), y, np.interp(y, trunk[:, 1], trunk[:, 2])])

    # ---- branch levels
    internode = sp.internode * (H / 20.0) ** 0.5
    internode = max(internode, 0.12)
    levels = []
    y = cb
    while y < H * 0.985:
        levels.append(y)
        y += internode * rng.uniform(0.75, 1.25)
    # also dead stubs below the crown (forest grown)
    stubs = []
    if ag["dead_low"] > 0 and cb > 2:
        ystub = cb * rng.uniform(0.35, 0.6)
        while ystub < cb:
            stubs.append(ystub)
            ystub += internode * rng.uniform(1.5, 4.0) * (1.5 if age == "old" else 1)

    branches = []
    az0 = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
    ai = 0
    # old-growth: crown built of irregular big limbs/clumps: skip many levels, vary lengths strongly
    lumps = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi, 3)
    for li, y in enumerate(levels):
        t = (y - cb) / (H - cb)
        n = rng.integers(sp.per_whorl[0], sp.per_whorl[1] + 1)
        rr = crown_radius(sp, t, rmax, round_top)
        for j in range(n):
            if sp.whorled:
                az = az0 + li * 0.6 + j * 2 * np.pi / n + rng.normal(0, 0.25)
            else:
                ai += 1
                az = az0 + ai * GOLD * 2.0 + rng.normal(0, 0.35)
            # envelope lobes: irregular crown outline
            lobe = 1 + 0.18 * irr * np.sin(az * 3 + lumps[0]) + 0.12 * irr * np.sin(az * 2 + lumps[1])
            L = rr * lobe * rng.uniform(1 - 0.35 * irr, 1 + 0.15 * irr)
            if age == "old" and rng.random() < 0.35 * irr and t < 0.85:
                continue  # gaps in old crowns
            if L < 0.05:
                continue
            # angle: upswept near top, drooping low
            ang = sp.base_angle + sp.top_upsweep * max(0, t - 0.5) * 2 - sp.low_droop * max(0, 0.5 - t) * 2
            ang += rng.normal(0, 0.08 + 0.1 * irr)
            dead = (t < ag["dead_low"] * 0.5 and rng.random() < 0.5)
            branches.append(_branch(trunk_at(y), az, ang, L, sp, rng, t, dead, H))
    for y in stubs:
        az = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi)
        L = rng.uniform(0.3, 1.2) * (H / 40) + 0.2
        b = _branch(trunk_at(y), az, rng.uniform(-0.4, 0.1), L, sp, rng, 0.0, True, H)
        branches.append(b)
    # old growth: a broken/candelabra top
    if ag["broken"] > 0 and rng.random() < ag["broken"] * 2:
        pass  # handled via leader snag in painter (spike top) -- see snag flag
    # ---- foliage
    fol = _foliage(branches, sp, rng, mpp, density_scale, H, age)
    # leader foliage: short sprays hugging the top of the stem
    axis = np.stack([trunk_at(y) for y in np.linspace(cb, H, 20)])
    rf = lambda yy: crown_radius(sp, (yy - cb) / (H - cb), rmax, round_top)
    return TreeGeom(sp, H, trunk, branches, fol, cb, axis, rf)


def _branch(p0, az, ang, L, sp, rng, t, dead, H):
    s = np.linspace(0, 1, 12)
    d = np.array([np.cos(az), 0, np.sin(az)])
    # height profile along the branch: initial angle, gravity droop, J upturn
    rise = np.sin(ang) * s * L
    sag = sp.droop * L * s ** 2 * (1 - 0.3 * t)
    up = sp.tip_upturn * L * np.clip(s - 0.55, 0, 1) ** 2 * 3.0
    horiz = np.cos(ang) * s * L
    wob = rng.normal(0, 0.02 * L, (12, 2)).cumsum(0) * 0.3
    pts = p0[None] + horiz[:, None] * d[None] + np.array([0, 1, 0])[None] * (rise - sag + up)[:, None]
    pts[:, 0] += wob[:, 0] * s
    pts[:, 2] += wob[:, 1] * s
    r0 = max(0.02, 0.018 * L + 0.004 * H * 0.05)
    return dict(pts=pts, r=r0, tier=t, dead=dead, az=az, L=L, dir=d)


def _foliage(branches, sp, rng, mpp, dens, H, age):
    P, N, D, PAD, BID, TIER, TIP = [], [], [], [], [], [], []
    # target spacing of foliage points: about half a pixel so masses are solid where dense
    step = max(0.45 * mpp, 0.02)
    for bi, b in enumerate(branches):
        if b["dead"]:
            continue
        L = b["L"]
        pts = b["pts"]
        s0 = sp.spray_start
        # clumps along the branch: spray groups
        nclump = max(1, int(L / max(0.35, 0.12 * H ** 0.5)) + 1)
        cs = np.sort(rng.uniform(s0, 1.0, nclump))
        # pad extent in metres
        wid = sp.pad_width * L
        hang = sp.pad_hang * L + 0.05
        top = sp.pad_top * L + 0.02
        # sample count ~ pad area / step^2 (volume shell) * density
        area = (1 - s0) * L * (2 * wid) * 1.0
        n = int(area / step ** 2 * 0.9 * sp.density * dens) + 8
        n = min(n, 6000)
        u = rng.uniform(s0, 1.0, n)
        # clumping: pull u toward clump centres
        if sp.clump > 0:
            ci = rng.integers(0, nclump, n)
            u = u * (1 - sp.clump * 0.7) + cs[ci] * sp.clump * 0.7 + rng.normal(0, 0.03, n)
            u = np.clip(u, s0, 1.0)
        # position along branch curve
        idx = u * (len(pts) - 1)
        i0 = np.floor(idx).astype(int).clip(0, len(pts) - 2)
        fr = (idx - i0)[:, None]
        c = pts[i0] * (1 - fr) + pts[i0 + 1] * fr
        tang = pts[i0 + 1] - pts[i0]
        tang /= np.linalg.norm(tang, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        side = np.cross(tang, np.array([0, 1, 0]))
        side /= np.linalg.norm(side, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        # width envelope: lanceolate pad, widest ~60% out, narrow at tip
        env = np.sin(np.clip((u - s0) / (1 - s0), 0, 1) * np.pi) ** 0.6 * (0.35 + 0.65 * (u - s0) / (1 - s0 + 1e-9))
        a = rng.uniform(-1, 1, n)
        lat = a * wid * env
        # vertical: flat spray at the top surface, pendant curtains below
        vr = rng.random(n)
        pend = rng.random(n) < sp.pendant * 0.6
        vy = np.where(pend, -vr ** 0.7 * hang * (0.4 + 0.6 * env), (vr - 0.6) * (top + 0.25 * hang) * env)
        # sprays droop at their outer edges (flat spray draped over the branch)
        vy = vy - (np.abs(a) ** 2) * hang * 0.5 * env
        p = c + side * lat[:, None] + np.array([0, 1, 0])[None] * vy[:, None]
        # pad coordinate: (across, vertical in pad, along)
        pv = vy / (hang + top)
        pad = np.stack([a * env, pv, u], 1)
        # spray normal: faces up, tilting outward at edges and down for pendant parts
        nrm = np.array([0, 1, 0])[None] * (1 - np.abs(a) * 0.5)[:, None] + side * (a * 0.6)[:, None] + tang * 0.25
        nrm = np.where(pend[:, None], side * np.sign(a)[:, None] * 0.7 + tang * 0.5 + np.array([0, 0.2, 0]), nrm)
        nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        # stroke direction
        if sp.stroke == "hang":
            dvec = np.array([0, -1, 0])[None] + tang * 0.25
        elif sp.stroke == "droop":
            dvec = tang * 0.6 + side * (np.sign(a) * 0.5)[:, None] + np.array([0, -0.55, 0])
        elif sp.stroke == "stiff":
            dvec = tang * 0.8 + side * (np.sign(a) * 0.4)[:, None] + np.array([0, -0.2, 0])
        else:  # brush
            dvec = tang * 0.5 + side * (np.sign(a) * 0.5)[:, None] + np.array([0, -0.4, 0])
        dvec = np.broadcast_to(dvec, (n, 3)).copy()
        dvec /= np.linalg.norm(dvec, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        P.append(p); N.append(nrm); D.append(dvec); PAD.append(pad)
        BID.append(np.full(n, bi)); TIER.append(np.full(n, b["tier"]))
        TIP.append(u)
    fol = dict(p=np.concatenate(P), n=np.concatenate(N), d=np.concatenate(D), pad=np.concatenate(PAD),
               bid=np.concatenate(BID), tier=np.concatenate(TIER), u=np.concatenate(TIP))
    return fol
Write: elements/conifers/render.py
"""Paint a TreeGeom into an index canvas (material, step).

Layers (after SYNTHESIS): form field (light per foliage point from crown/pad/spray normals, an
opacity shadow map and crown depth), a value plan with two families kept apart, marks (points drawn
as short strokes in the species direction, a light cap on each stroke), and quantization with
dither that mixes only adjacent steps.
"""
import numpy as np

FOL, BARK, WOOD = 1, 2, 3
NSTEP = 8          # foliage steps: 0..3 shadow family, 4..7 lit family


def sun_vec(az_deg, el_deg):
    """az measured in the picture: 0 = sun from the viewer's left, 90 = behind-left... we use
    az: 0 left, 90 toward viewer (front), 180 right, 270 behind."""
    a = np.radians(az_deg); e = np.radians(el_deg)
    return np.array([-np.cos(a) * np.cos(e), np.sin(e), np.sin(a) * np.cos(e)])


class Cam:
    def __init__(self, mpp, el_deg=0.0, yaw_deg=0.0):
        self.mpp = mpp
        self.el = np.radians(el_deg)
        self.yaw = np.radians(yaw_deg)

    def rot(self, p):
        c, s = np.cos(self.yaw), np.sin(self.yaw)
        x = p[..., 0] * c + p[..., 2] * s
        z = -p[..., 0] * s + p[..., 2] * c
        return x, p[..., 1], z

    def project(self, p):
        x, y, z = self.rot(p)
        ce, se = np.cos(self.el), np.sin(self.el)
        sx = x / self.mpp
        sy = -(y * ce - z * se) / self.mpp
        depth = y * se + z * ce
        return sx, sy, depth

    def rotvec(self, d):
        x, y, z = self.rot(d)
        ce, se = np.cos(self.el), np.sin(self.el)
        return x, -(y * ce - z * se)


def shadow_map(p, L, cell, k=0.35, blur=1):
    """Opacity shadow: for each point, how many foliage points lie between it and the sun
    (binned on a grid perpendicular to the sun). Returns transmittance in (0,1]."""
    up = np.array([0, 1.0, 0]) if abs(L[1]) < 0.95 else np.array([1.0, 0, 0])
    e1 = np.cross(L, up); e1 /= np.linalg.norm(e1)
    e2 = np.cross(L, e1)
    u = p @ e1; v = p @ e2; w = p @ L
    iu = np.floor((u - u.min()) / cell).astype(np.int64)
    iv = np.floor((v - v.min()) / cell).astype(np.int64)
    nu, nv = iu.max() + 1, iv.max() + 1
    key = iu * nv + iv
    order = np.lexsort((-w, key))           # per cell, sunward first
    ks = key[order]
    # rank within cell = number of points sunward of this one
    start = np.r_[0, np.flatnonzero(np.diff(ks)) + 1]
    counts = np.diff(np.r_[start, len(ks)])
    rank = np.arange(len(ks)) - np.repeat(start, counts)
    before = np.empty(len(p)); before[order] = rank
    # soften: average the rank with neighbours' column occupancy
    if blur:
        grid = np.zeros((nu, nv)); np.add.at(grid, (iu, iv), 1)
        from scipy.ndimage import uniform_filter
        gb = uniform_filter(grid, 3)
        frac = before / np.maximum(grid[iu, iv], 1)
        before = frac * gb[iu, iv] * 0.6 + before * 0.4
    return np.exp(-k * before * (cell / 0.25) ** 0.0)


def light_foliage(tg, L, mpp, sky=0.35, wc=0.55, shadow_k=None, rng=None):
    f = tg.fol
    p, n = f["p"], f["n"]
    # crown normal: radial from the crown axis, tilted up by the cone slope
    ax = tg.crown_axis
    axx = np.interp(p[:, 1], ax[:, 1], ax[:, 0]); axz = np.interp(p[:, 1], ax[:, 1], ax[:, 2])
    rad = np.stack([p[:, 0] - axx, np.zeros(len(p)), p[:, 2] - axz], 1)
    rdist = np.linalg.norm(rad, axis=1)
    rad /= rdist[:, None] + 1e-9
    rf = np.maximum(tg.rfun(p[:, 1]), 0.05)
    slope = 0.35
    nc = rad * np.cos(slope) + np.array([0, np.sin(slope), 0])
    # crown top faces up
    nc[:, 1] += np.clip((p[:, 1] - (tg.H - 0.15 * (tg.H - tg.crown_base))) / (0.15 * (tg.H - tg.crown_base)), 0, 1)
    nc /= np.linalg.norm(nc, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    N = wc * nc + (1 - wc) * n
    N /= np.linalg.norm(N, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    lam = np.clip(N @ L, 0, 1)
    # cast shadow inside the crown
    cell = max(mpp * 2.0, 0.25)
    k = shadow_k if shadow_k is not None else 0.5 * (mpp / cell) ** 2 * 3
    T = shadow_map(p, L, cell, k=k)
    # depth inside crown envelope -> ambient occlusion
    depth = np.clip(1 - rdist / rf, 0, 1)
    ao = np.clip(1 - depth * 1.1, 0.05, 1) ** 1.2
    pad_v = f["pad"][:, 1]
    ao *= np.clip(0.75 + 0.5 * pad_v, 0.3, 1.1)      # undersides of pads darker
    direct = lam * T
    amb = sky * ao * (0.55 + 0.45 * N[:, 1])
    return direct, amb, T, ao, lam


def rasterize(tg, cam, L, W, Hpx, origin, params):
    """Return G-buffer dict: mat, val (direct), amb, depth, fam, plus a stroke-pos field."""
    mpp = cam.mpp
    rng = np.random.default_rng(params.get("seed", 0) + 17)
    f = tg.fol
    direct, amb, T, ao, lam = light_foliage(tg, L, mpp, sky=params.get("sky", 0.35),
                                            wc=params.get("wc", 0.55), shadow_k=params.get("shadow_k"))
    sx, sy, dep = cam.project(f["p"])
    sx = sx + origin[0]; sy = sy + origin[1]
    # strokes: length in px from the spray-mark size in metres
    mark_m = params.get("mark_m", 0.35)
    slen = np.clip(mark_m / mpp, 1, params.get("max_stroke", 4))
    dx, dy = cam.rotvec(f["d"])
    nrm = np.sqrt(dx ** 2 + dy ** 2) + 1e-9
    dx /= nrm; dy /= nrm
    nseg = int(np.ceil(slen.max()))
    buf_mat = np.zeros((Hpx, W), np.int16)
    buf_dep = np.full((Hpx, W), -1e9)
    buf_dir = np.zeros((Hpx, W)); buf_amb = np.zeros((Hpx, W))
    buf_cap = np.zeros((Hpx, W), np.int8)      # 1 = first pixel of a stroke (light cap)
    buf_id = np.full((Hpx, W), -1, np.int64)
    buf_bid = np.full((Hpx, W), -1, np.int64)
    XS, YS, DS, VD, VA, CAP, ID = [], [], [], [], [], [], []
    ids = np.arange(len(sx))
    for k in range(nseg):
        m = slen > k
        xs = np.floor(sx[m] + dx[m] * k + 0.5).astype(int)
        ys = np.floor(sy[m] + dy[m] * k + 0.5).astype(int)
        XS.append(xs); YS.append(ys); DS.append(dep[m] - k * 1e-4)
        VD.append(direct[m]); VA.append(amb[m]); CAP.append(np.full(m.sum(), 1 if k == 0 else 0)); ID.append(ids[m])
    xs = np.concatenate(XS); ys = np.concatenate(YS); ds = np.concatenate(DS)
    vd = np.concatenate(VD); va = np.concatenate(VA); cap = np.concatenate(CAP); pid = np.concatenate(ID)
    ok = (xs >= 0) & (xs < W) & (ys >= 0) & (ys < Hpx)
    xs, ys, ds, vd, va, cap, pid = xs[ok], ys[ok], ds[ok], vd[ok], va[ok], cap[ok], pid[ok]
    o = np.argsort(ds)                     # far first, near last wins
    xs, ys, ds, vd, va, cap, pid = xs[o], ys[o], ds[o], vd[o], va[o], cap[o], pid[o]
    buf_mat[ys, xs] = FOL
    buf_dep[ys, xs] = ds
    buf_dir[ys, xs] = vd
    buf_amb[ys, xs] = va
    buf_cap[ys, xs] = cap
    buf_id[ys, xs] = pid
    buf_bid[ys, xs] = f["bid"][pid]
    G = dict(mat=buf_mat, dep=buf_dep, dir=buf_dir, amb=buf_amb, cap=buf_cap, pid=buf_id, bid=buf_bid)
    _wood(tg, cam, L, G, origin, params)
    return G


def _wood(tg, cam, L, G, origin, params):
    """Trunk and branches, drawn behind/among foliage by depth."""
    mpp = cam.mpp
    W = G["mat"].shape[1]; Hpx = G["mat"].shape[0]
    tr = tg.trunk
    # trunk as a stack of discs sampled on the visible half-cylinder
    ys = np.arange(0, tg.H, mpp * 0.5)
    X = np.interp(ys, tr[:, 1], tr[:, 0]); Z = np.interp(ys, tr[:, 1], tr[:, 2]); R = np.interp(ys, tr[:, 1], tr[:, 3])
    pts, nrms, us = [], [], []
    for yy, xx, zz, rr in zip(ys, X, Z, R):
        na = max(6, int(np.pi * rr / (mpp * 0.5)))
        th = np.linspace(-np.pi / 2, np.pi / 2, na)   # front half facing viewer (+z) after yaw ~0
        th = th + cam.yaw
        nx, nz = np.sin(th), np.cos(th)
        pts.append(np.stack([xx + rr * nx, np.full(na, yy), zz + rr * nz], 1))
        nrms.append(np.stack([nx, np.zeros(na), nz], 1))
        us.append(th - cam.yaw)
    P = np.concatenate(pts); Nn = np.concatenate(nrms); U = np.concatenate(us)
    sx, sy, dep = cam.project(P)
    xs = np.floor(sx + origin[0] + 0.5).astype(int); ysx = np.floor(sy + origin[1] + 0.5).astype(int)
    lam = np.clip(Nn @ L, 0, 1)
    ok = (xs >= 0) & (xs < W) & (ysx >= 0) & (ysx < Hpx)
    xs, ysx, dep, lam, U, Pk = xs[ok], ysx[ok], dep[ok], lam[ok], U[ok], P[ok]
    o = np.argsort(dep)
    xs, ysx, dep, lam, U, Pk = xs[o], ysx[o], dep[o], lam[o], U[o], Pk[o]
    front = dep > G["dep"][ysx, xs]
    xs, ysx, dep, lam, U, Pk = xs[front], ysx[front], dep[front], lam[front], U[front], Pk[front]
    G["mat"][ysx, xs] = BARK
    G["dep"][ysx, xs] = dep
    G["dir"][ysx, xs] = lam
    G["amb"][ysx, xs] = 0.3
    G.setdefault("bark_u", np.zeros_like(G["dir"]))
    G.setdefault("bark_y", np.zeros_like(G["dir"]))
    G["bark_u"][ysx, xs] = U
    G["bark_y"][ysx, xs] = Pk[:, 1]
    # branches: 1px lines where thick enough to show
    for b in tg.branches:
        rpx = b["r"] / mpp
        if rpx < 0.15 and not b["dead"]:
            continue
        pts = b["pts"]
        # dense sample
        s = np.linspace(0, 1, max(4, int(b["L"] / mpp * 1.5)))
        idx = s * (len(pts) - 1)
        i0 = np.floor(idx).astype(int).clip(0, len(pts) - 2)
        fr = (idx - i0)[:, None]
        c = pts[i0] * (1 - fr) + pts[i0 + 1] * fr
        if not b["dead"]:
            c = c[s < 0.8]
        sx, sy, dep = cam.project(c)
        xs = np.floor(sx + origin[0] + 0.5).astype(int); ysx = np.floor(sy + origin[1] + 0.5).astype(int)
        ok = (xs >= 0) & (xs < W) & (ysx >= 0) & (ysx < Hpx)
        xs, ysx, dep = xs[ok], ysx[ok], dep[ok]
        front = dep > G["dep"][ysx, xs]
        G["mat"][ysx[front], xs[front]] = WOOD
        G["dep"][ysx[front], xs[front]] = dep[front]
        G["dir"][ysx[front], xs[front]] = 0.2 if b["dead"] else 0.0
        G["amb"][ysx[front], xs[front]] = 0.2


def noise2(shape, seed, scale=1.0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = rng.random(shape)
    if scale > 1:
        from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
        a = gaussian_filter(a, scale)
        a = (a - a.min()) / (a.ptp() + 1e-9)
    return a


def quantize(G, params):
    """Continuous light -> foliage step 0..7 with the family boundary kept hard."""
    mat = G["mat"]
    d, a = G["dir"], G["amb"]
    thr = params.get("lit_thr", 0.18)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(params.get("seed", 0) + 5)
    Hh, W = mat.shape
    jitter = rng.random((Hh, W))
    step = np.zeros((Hh, W), np.int16)
    lit = d > thr
    # lit family 4..7 by direct light; shadow family 0..3 by ambient
    lv = np.clip((d - thr) / (params.get("lit_span", 0.55)), 0, 0.999)
    sv = np.clip(a / params.get("amb_span", 0.4), 0, 0.999)
    dith = params.get("dither", 0.5)
    ls = np.floor(lv * 4 + (jitter - 0.5) * dith).clip(0, 3)
    ss = np.floor(sv * 4 + (jitter - 0.5) * dith).clip(0, 3)
    step = np.where(lit, 4 + ls, ss).astype(np.int16)
    # light cap: first pixel of each stroke one step up (within its family)
    if params.get("cap", True):
        cap = G["cap"] == 1
        up = np.where(lit, np.minimum(step + 1, 7), np.minimum(step + 1, 3))
        step = np.where(cap & (rng.random((Hh, W)) < params.get("cap_p", 0.5)), up, step)
    step[mat == 0] = -1
    return step


def compose(step, mat, G, pal, bg):
    """Colours from palette dict: pal['fol'][step], pal['bark'][step], pal['wood']."""
    Hh, W = step.shape
    out = bg.copy()
    m = mat == FOL
    out[m] = pal["fol"][step[m]]
    m = mat == BARK
    out[m] = pal["bark"][np.clip(step[m], 0, len(pal["bark"]) - 1)]
    m = mat == WOOD
    out[m] = pal["wood"]
    return out

    """G-buffer: foliage body (every point, 1 px) + marks (a sparse subset drawn as strokes in
    the species direction, slightly in front of their own spray). Marks carry the light; the
    body is the calm mass between them."""
    mpp = cam.mpp
    rng = np.random.default_rng(params.get("seed", 0) + 17)
    f = tg.fol
    direct, amb, T, ao, lam = light_foliage(tg, L, mpp, sky=params.get("sky", 0.35),
                                            wc=params.get("wc", 0.55), shadow_k=params.get("shadow_k"))

    sx = sx + origin[0]; sy = sy + origin[1]
    N = len(sx)
    mark_m = params.get("mark_m", 0.35)
    Ls = float(np.clip(mark_m / mpp, 1, params.get("max_stroke", 4)))
    slen = np.clip(np.round(Ls * rng.uniform(0.6, 1.3, N)), 1, params.get("max_stroke", 4))
    is_mark = rng.random(N) < params.get("mark_frac", 0.25)

    ids = np.arange(N)

        XS.append(np.floor(sx[m] + dx[m] * k + 0.5).astype(int))

        K.append(np.full(m.sum(), k)); ID.append(ids[m])

    kk = np.concatenate(K); pid = np.concatenate(ID)
    ok = (xs >= 0) & (xs < W) & (ys >= 0) & (ys < Hpx)
    xs, ys, ds, kk, pid = xs[ok], ys[ok], ds[ok], kk[ok], pid[ok]

    G["pid"][ys, xs] = pid
    G["bid"][ys, xs] = f["bid"][pid]
    G["T"][ys, xs] = T[pid]
    _wood(tg, cam, L, G, origin, params)
    return G

    """Continuous light -> foliage step 0..7 with the family boundary kept hard.
    Marks: lit family 5..7 when in direct light, else dim marks 2..3 in the shadow family.
    Body: shadow family 0..2 by ambient; lit body only where direct light is strong (step 4..5)."""
    mat = G["mat"]
    d, a = G["dir"], G["amb"]
    thr = params.get("lit_thr", 0.18)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(params.get("seed", 0) + 5)
    Hh, W = mat.shape
    jit = rng.random((Hh, W)) - 0.5
    dith = params.get("dither", 0.6)
    lv = np.clip((d - thr) / params.get("lit_span", 0.55), 0, 0.999)
    sv = np.clip(a / params.get("amb_span", 0.4), 0, 0.999)
    mark = G["mark"]
    lit = d > thr
    body_lit = d > params.get("body_thr", 0.45)
    # marks
    m_lit = 5 + np.floor(lv * 3 + jit * dith).clip(0, 2)
    m_dim = 1 + np.floor(sv * 3 + jit * dith).clip(0, 2)
    # body
    b_lit = 4 + np.floor(np.clip((d - params.get("body_thr", 0.45)) / 0.4, 0, 0.999) * 2 + jit * dith).clip(0, 1)
    b_sh = np.floor(sv * 3 + jit * dith).clip(0, 2)
    step = np.where(mark, np.where(lit, m_lit, m_dim), np.where(body_lit, b_lit, b_sh)).astype(np.int16)
    # the tail of a lit stroke is one step darker than its head (light cap on top)
    tail = mark & (G["k"] > 0) & lit & (rng.random((Hh, W)) < params.get("tail_p", 0.5))
    step = np.where(tail, np.maximum(step - 1, 4), step)
    bark = mat == BARK
    step = np.where(bark, np.floor(np.clip(d * 0.8 + a, 0, 0.999) * 6 + jit * dith).clip(0, 5), step).astype(np.int16)
    step[mat == 0] = -1
    return step

"""Side-by-side comparison helpers: Ferrari crop | mine at kx, labelled."""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
def up(a,k): return np.repeat(np.repeat(a,k,0),k,1)
def panel(imgs, labels, k=8, gap=6, bg=(250,250,250)):
    ims=[up(np.asarray(i)[..., :3],k) for i in imgs]
    H=max(i.shape[0] for i in ims)+16; W=sum(i.shape[1] for i in ims)+gap*(len(ims)-1)
    out=np.full((H,W,3),bg,np.uint8); x=0
    for i in ims:
        out[16:16+i.shape[0],x:x+i.shape[1]]=i; x+=i.shape[1]+gap
    im=Image.fromarray(out); d=ImageDraw.Draw(im); x=0
    for i,l in zip(ims,labels):
        d.text((x+2,2),l,fill=(0,0,0)); x+=i.shape[1]+gap
    return im
def stack(ims, gap=6, bg=(250,250,250)):
    ims=[np.asarray(i) for i in ims]
    W=max(i.shape[1] for i in ims); H=sum(i.shape[0] for i in ims)+gap*(len(ims)-1)
    out=np.full((H,W,3),bg,np.uint8); y=0
    for i in ims: out[y:y+i.shape[0],:i.shape[1]]=i[...,:3]; y+=i.shape[0]+gap
    return Image.fromarray(out)
EOF
echo ok

Bash: Stage 0 copy harness first run
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/conifers; cat > s0_copy.py <<'EOF'
"""Stage 0: master copy of Ferrari's V16 tarn conifer (C1: the mid-distance spire, native (420,30)-(480,130))."""
import numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
import tree, render as R, lw, cmp
d=lw.load('V16'); pal=d['pal']; idx=d['idx']
FOL=[62,61,60,59,63,73,58,54]
P=dict(fol=pal[FOL], bark=pal[[62,61,60,59,63,73]], wood=pal[61])
X0,Y0,X1,Y1=420,30,480,130
ref=pal[idx[Y0:Y1,X0:X1]]
def skybg(x0,y0,x1,y1):
    sub=idx[y0:y1,x0:x1]; out=np.zeros(sub.shape+(3,),np.uint8)
    for r in range(sub.shape[0]):
        row=idx[y0+r, 300:420]; sky=row[(row>=1)&(row<=17)]
        v=np.bincount(sky).argmax() if len(sky) else 9
        out[r]=pal[v]
    # keep his checker seams: copy sky pixels where they are sky
    m=(sub>=1)&(sub<=17); out[m]=pal[sub[m]]
    return out
def run(params, seed=3, H=24.0, mpp=0.1, sun=(15,30), tag='v'):
    tg=tree.build('subalpine','young',seed=seed,H=H,mpp=mpp)
    cam=R.Cam(mpp,0)
    W,Hh=X1-X0, Y1-Y0
    # tree top at row ~3 of the crop, centred at x ~ 29
    Hpx=int(H/mpp)+10
    G=R.rasterize(tg,cam,R.sun_vec(*sun),W,Hpx,(29,Hpx-4),params)
    st=R.quantize(G,params)
    top=np.argmax((G['mat']>0).any(1))
    bg=np.zeros((Hpx,W,3),np.uint8); bg[:]=pal[9]
    img=R.compose(st,G['mat'],G,P,bg)
    crop=img[top-3:top-3+Hh]
    msk=(G['mat'][top-3:top-3+Hh]>0)
    out=skybg(X0,Y0,X1,Y1); out[msk]=crop[msk]
    return out
if __name__=='__main__':
    params=dict(seed=1, mark_m=0.3, mark_frac=0.3, lit_thr=0.2, body_thr=0.5)
    mine=run(params)
    cmp.panel([ref,mine],['Ferrari V16 C1 8x','copy 8x'],k=8).save('stage0/c1_v1.png')
EOF
OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 nice -n 10 python3 s0_copy.py

Bash: Class shares and run lengths, Ferrari vs mine
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/conifers; cat > stats.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
CLS={'black':[62,61,60,71],'dgreen':[59,72,70],'dolive':[73,63,55],'lit':[58,54]}
def classify_idx(sub):
    c=np.full(sub.shape,-1)
    for i,(k,v) in enumerate(CLS.items()): c[np.isin(sub,v)]=i
    return c
def classify_rgb(img,pal):
    out=np.full(img.shape[:2],-1)
    for i,(k,v) in enumerate(CLS.items()):
        for j in v: out[(img==pal[j]).all(-1)]=i
    return out
def runs(c,val):
    L=[]
    for row in c:
        m=(row==val).astype(int); d=np.diff(np.r_[0,m,0]); s=np.flatnonzero(d==1); e=np.flatnonzero(d==-1); L+=list(e-s)
    return np.mean(L) if L else 0
def vruns(c,val): return runs(c.T,val)
def report(c,name):
    tot=(c>=0).sum()
    s=f'{name:10s} n={tot:5d} '
    for i,k in enumerate(CLS):
        s+=f'{k}:{(c==i).sum()/tot:.2f} (h{runs(c,i):.1f} v{vruns(c,i):.1f})  '
    print(s)
EOF

Bash: Density mixture quantization and tune
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/conifers; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('render.py').read()
a=s.index('def quantize(')
b=s.index('def compose(')
newq='''def quantize(G, params):
    """Continuous light -> foliage step.
    Foliage uses a *density mixture*: value v (direct light + ambient + a bias for mark pixels)
    is quantized with a wide stochastic dither, so a mass is a fine intermix of adjacent steps
    whose proportions carry the form (Ferrari's mid-distance conifer: black 47%, dark olive 23%,
    lit 27%, mean runs ~1.5 px). The family boundary is kept: pixels under lit_thr can never
    take lit-family steps; lit pixels mix within 0/4..7 (black glimpses of the interior allowed).
    """
    mat = G["mat"]
    d, a = G["dir"], G["amb"]
    thr = params.get("lit_thr", 0.18)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(params.get("seed", 0) + 5)
    Hh, W = mat.shape
    noise = rng.random((Hh, W))
    amp = params.get("mix", 1.2)
    mark = G["mark"]
    lit = d > thr
    # lit family: levels [black(0), 4, 5, 6, 7]
    lv = np.clip((d - thr) / params.get("lit_span", 0.5), 0, 1) + np.where(mark, params.get("mark_bias", 0.25), -0.05)
    q = np.floor(lv * 4 + (noise - 0.5) * amp + params.get("lit_off", 0.3)).clip(0, 4).astype(np.int16)
    lit_levels = np.array(params.get("lit_levels", [0, 4, 5, 6, 7]), np.int16)
    s_lit = lit_levels[q]
    # shadow family 0..3 by ambient
    sv = np.clip(a / params.get("amb_span", 0.35), 0, 1) + np.where(mark, 0.15, 0)
    q2 = np.floor(sv * 3 + (noise - 0.5) * amp * 0.8 - 0.4).clip(0, 3).astype(np.int16)
    step = np.where(lit, s_lit, q2).astype(np.int16)
    bark = mat == BARK
    step = np.where(bark, np.floor(np.clip(d * 0.8 + a, 0, 0.999) * 6 + (noise - .5) * 0.6).clip(0, 5), step).astype(np.int16)
    step[mat == 0] = -1
    return step


'''
s=s[:a]+newq+s[b:]
open('render.py','w').write(s)
EOF

Bash: Stochastic three-way mixture quantization
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/conifers; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('render.py').read()
a=s.index('    # lit family: levels [black(0), 4, 5, 6, 7]')
b=s.index('    bark = mat == BARK')
new='''    # lit family: stochastic 3-way mixture black / dark olive / lit with probabilities set by v.
    v = np.clip((d - thr) / params.get("lit_span", 0.5), 0, 1)
    v = np.clip(v + np.where(mark, params.get("mark_bias", 0.2), -0.05), 0, 1)
    pl = params.get("p_lit", 0.62) * v ** params.get("g_lit", 0.9)
    pd = params.get("p_dol", 0.45) * (1 - 0.5 * v) * np.clip(v * 3 + 0.3, 0, 1)
    r = noise
    s_lit = np.where(r < pl, np.where(rng.random((Hh, W)) < v * 0.6, 7, 6),
                     np.where(r < pl + pd, np.where(rng.random((Hh, W)) < 0.5, 5, 4), 0))
    # shadow family: mostly black with dim flecks (dark green / near-black) in the ambient
    sv = np.clip(a / params.get("amb_span", 0.35), 0, 1)
    ps = params.get("p_fleck", 0.25) * sv + np.where(mark, 0.08, 0)
    s_sh = np.where(r < ps, np.where(rng.random((Hh, W)) < 0.5, 3, 2), np.where(r < ps * 2.2, 1, 0))
    step = np.where(lit, s_lit, s_sh).astype(np.int16)
'''
s=s[:a]+new+s[b:]
open('render.py','w').write(s)

"for mix,off in [(0.5,0.4),(0.62,0.45),(0.75,0.4)]:\n    params=dict(seed=1, mark_m=0.2, mark_frac=0.4, lit_thr=0.12, shadow_k=0.5, p_lit=mix, p_dol=off)")

Bash: Add spur lines to branches
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/conifers; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('tree.py').read()
old='''        P.append(p); N.append(nrm); D.append(dvec); PAD.append(pad)
        BID.append(np.full(n, bi)); TIER.append(np.full(n, b["tier"]))
        TIP.append(u)'''
new='''        # spur: the twig axis of the outer branch, needled, reads as the horizontal spur line
        if sp.spur > 0:
            ns = int(sp.spur * (1 - 0.5) * L / step) + 2
            us = np.sort(rng.uniform(0.5, 1.0, ns))
            idx2 = us * (len(pts) - 1)
            j0 = np.floor(idx2).astype(int).clip(0, len(pts) - 2)
            f2 = (idx2 - j0)[:, None]
            cs2 = pts[j0] * (1 - f2) + pts[j0 + 1] * f2 + rng.normal(0, step * 0.4, (ns, 3))
            tg2 = pts[j0 + 1] - pts[j0]; tg2 /= np.linalg.norm(tg2, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
            p = np.concatenate([p, cs2]); 
            nrm = np.concatenate([nrm, np.tile([0, 0.9, 0.3], (ns, 1)) / 0.95])
            dvec = np.concatenate([dvec, tg2])
            pad = np.concatenate([pad, np.stack([np.zeros(ns), np.full(ns, 0.3), us], 1)])
            u = np.concatenate([u, us]); n = n + ns
        P.append(p); N.append(nrm); D.append(dvec); PAD.append(pad)
        BID.append(np.full(n, bi)); TIER.append(np.full(n, b["tier"]))
        TIP.append(u)'''
s=s.replace(old,new)
s=s.replace('''    trunk_frac: float = 0.012       # dbh radius / height (young)''','''    trunk_frac: float = 0.012       # dbh radius / height (young)
    spur: float = 0.0               # density of needled twig-axis points (spur lines at the edge)''')
s=s.replace('''trunk_frac=0.007),''','''trunk_frac=0.007, spur=1.0),''')
open('tree.py','w').write(s)
EOF

Bash: Needled leader, wider spire
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/conifers; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('tree.py').read()
s=s.replace('"subalpine": Species("subalpine", crown_pow=1.45, crown_ratio=0.9, rmax_frac=0.24,','"subalpine": Species("subalpine", crown_pow=1.25, crown_ratio=0.9, rmax_frac=0.27,')
old='''    # ---- foliage
    fol = _foliage(branches, sp, rng, mpp, density_scale, H, age)'''
new='''    # ---- foliage
    fol = _foliage(branches, sp, rng, mpp, density_scale, H, age)
    # the leader is needled: a thin column of foliage points up the top of the stem
    ltop = max(0.08 * H, 3 * mpp)
    nl = int(ltop / (0.4 * mpp)) + 2
    yl = rng.uniform(H - ltop, H * 0.995, nl)
    cl = np.stack([trunk_at(y) for y in yl]) + rng.normal(0, 0.25 * mpp, (nl, 3)) * np.array([1, 0, 1])
    fol["p"] = np.concatenate([fol["p"], cl])
    fol["n"] = np.concatenate([fol["n"], np.tile([0, 0.6, 0.8], (nl, 1))])
    fol["d"] = np.concatenate([fol["d"], np.tile([0, -1.0, 0], (nl, 1))])
    fol["pad"] = np.concatenate([fol["pad"], np.tile([0, 0.5, 1.0], (nl, 1))])
    fol["bid"] = np.concatenate([fol["bid"], np.full(nl, -1)])
    fol["tier"] = np.concatenate([fol["tier"], np.ones(nl)])
    fol["u"] = np.concatenate([fol["u"], np.ones(nl)])'''
s=s.replace(old,new)
open('tree.py','w').write(s)
EOF
