def _foliage(branches, sp, rng, mpp, detail, H, age, elscale=1.0):
    out = {k: [] for k in ("p", "n", "d", "pad", "bid", "eid", "ek", "kind")}
    step = max(0.45 * mpp / detail, 0.015)
    eid = 0
    has_child = set(b.get("parent", -1) for b in branches)
    for bi, b in enumerate(branches):
        if b["dead"]:
            continue
        L = b["L"]; pts = b["pts"]
        s0 = sp.spray_start if not b["child"] else 0.1
        if bi in has_child:
            s0 = max(s0, 0.4)        # a fanned limb carries foliage mostly on its children and tip
        flen = (1 - s0) * L
        spacing = max(sp.spacing, 0.5 * mpp)
        na = max(2, int(flen / spacing))
        us = np.sort(rng.uniform(s0, 1.0, na))
        c = np.array([_pt(pts, u) for u in us])
        tang = np.gradient(c, axis=0) if na > 2 else np.tile(pts[-1] - pts[0], (na, 1))
        tang /= np.linalg.norm(tang, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        side = np.cross(tang, [0, 1.0, 0]); side /= np.linalg.norm(side, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        radial = b["dir"]
        # element size tapers toward the twig tip (young growth at the tip is short)
        xx = (us - s0) / max(1 - s0, 1e-3)
        taper = (np.clip(1 - xx, 0, 1) ** sp.wedge * 0.9 + 0.18) * elscale     # wedge: long sprays inboard, short at the tip
        wsum = sp.frond_w + sp.strand_w + sp.brush_w
        for i in range(na):
            r = rng.random() * wsum
            kind = "frond" if r < sp.frond_w else ("strand" if r < sp.frond_w + sp.strand_w else "brush")
            for sgn in (-1, 1):
                if kind == "strand" and sgn == 1 and rng.random() < 0.5:
                    continue
                el = _element(kind, c[i], tang[i], side[i] * sgn, radial, sp, rng, step, taper[i], L)
                if el is None:
                    continue
                P, Nn, D, ek = el
                m = len(P)
                out["p"].append(P); out["n"].append(Nn); out["d"].append(D)
                out["ek"].append(ek)
                across = np.full(m, sgn * 0.5)
                vert = (P[:, 1] - c[i][1]) / (sp.strand_len + sp.frond_len + 0.1)
                out["pad"].append(np.stack([across, vert, np.full(m, us[i])], 1))
                out["bid"].append(np.full(m, bi)); out["eid"].append(np.full(m, eid)); out["kind"].append(np.full(m, KIND[kind]))
                eid += 1
        # spur: the needled twig tip, reads as the horizontal spur at the silhouette
        ns = max(2, int(0.35 * L / step))
        su = np.linspace(0.65, 1.0, ns)
        P = np.array([_pt(pts, u) for u in su]) + rng.normal(0, step * 0.3, (ns, 3))
        Dd = np.tile(tang[-1], (ns, 1))
        Nn = np.tile(np.array([0, 0.85, 0]) + 0.5 * radial, (ns, 1))
        Nn /= np.linalg.norm(Nn, axis=1, keepdims=True)
        out["p"].append(P); out["n"].append(Nn); out["d"].append(Dd); out["ek"].append((su - 0.65) / 0.35)
        out["pad"].append(np.stack([np.zeros(ns), np.full(ns, 0.2), su], 1))
        out["bid"].append(np.full(n
Write: elements/conifers/foliage_fast.py
"""Vectorized foliage elements (same rules as grow._foliage_slow / grow._element, ~10-20x faster).

All anchors of all branches are gathered first; each element kind is then generated for every
element at once with ragged-array (repeat / cumsum) indexing.
"""
import numpy as np

UP = np.array([0, 1.0, 0])
KIND = {"frond": 0, "strand": 1, "brush": 2, "spur": 3, "leader": 4}


def _nrm(v):
    return v / (np.linalg.norm(v, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9)


def _ragged(n):
    """element index and local index for ragged lengths n."""
    n = np.asarray(n, int)
    e = np.repeat(np.arange(len(n)), n)
    start = np.cumsum(n) - n
    k = np.arange(n.sum()) - start[e]
    return e, k


def _pts_at(pts, u):
    idx = u * (len(pts) - 1)
    i0 = np.clip(np.floor(idx).astype(int), 0, len(pts) - 2)
    f = (idx - i0)[:, None]
    return pts[i0] * (1 - f) + pts[i0 + 1] * f


def foliage(branches, sp, rng, mpp, detail, H, age, elscale=1.0):
    step = max(0.45 * mpp / detail, 0.015)
    has_child = set(b.get("parent", -1) for b in branches)
    # ---- gather elements
    EC, ET, ES, ER, ETAP, EU, EB, EK, ESG, EY = [], [], [], [], [], [], [], [], [], []
    spur = []
    wsum = sp.frond_w + sp.strand_w + sp.brush_w
    for bi, b in enumerate(branches):
        if b["dead"]:
            continue
        L = b["L"]; pts = b["pts"]
        s0 = sp.spray_start if not b["child"] else 0.1
        if bi in has_child:
            s0 = max(s0, 0.4)
        spacing = max(sp.spacing, 0.5 * mpp)
        na = max(2, int((1 - s0) * L / spacing))
        us = np.sort(rng.uniform(s0, 1.0, na))
        c = _pts_at(pts, us)
        tang = np.gradient(c, axis=0) if na > 2 else np.tile(pts[-1] - pts[0], (na, 1))
        tang = _nrm(tang)
        side = _nrm(np.cross(tang, UP))
        xx = (us - s0) / max(1 - s0, 1e-3)
        taper = (np.clip(1 - xx, 0, 1) ** sp.wedge * 0.9 + 0.18) * elscale
        r = rng.random(na) * wsum
        kind = np.where(r < sp.frond_w, 0, np.where(r < sp.frond_w + sp.strand_w, 1, 2))
        for sg in (-1, 1):
            keep = ~((kind == 1) & (sg == 1) & (rng.random(na) < 0.5))
            m = keep.sum()
            EC.append(c[keep]); ET.append(tang[keep]); ES.append(side[keep] * sg); ER.append(np.tile(b["dir"], (m, 1)))
            ETAP.append(taper[keep]); EU.append(us[keep]); EB.append(np.full(m, bi)); EK.append(kind[keep])
            ESG.append(np.full(m, sg)); EY.append(c[keep][:, 1])
        ns = max(2, int(0.35 * L / step))
        su = np.linspace(0.65, 1.0, ns)
        spur.append((bi, _pts_at(pts, su) + rng.normal(0, step * 0.3, (ns, 3)), tang[-1], b["dir"], su))
    if not EC:
        z = np.zeros((0, 3))
        return dict(p=z, n=z, d=z, pad=z, bid=np.zeros(0, int), eid=np.zeros(0, int), ek=np.zeros(0), kind=np.zeros(0, int))
    C = np.concatenate(EC); T = np.concatenate(ET); S = np.concatenate(ES); R = np.concatenate(ER)
    TAP = np.concatenate(ETAP); U = np.concatenate(EU); B = np.concatenate(EB); K = np.concatenate(EK)
    SG = np.concatenate(ESG)
    NE = len(C)
    eids = np.arange(NE)
    parts = []
    for kind, gen in ((0, _fronds), (1, _strands), (2, _brush)):
        sel = np.flatnonzero(K == kind)
        if len(sel) == 0:
            continue
        P, N, D, ek, e = gen(C[sel], T[sel], S[sel], R[sel], TAP[sel], sp, rng, step)
        P, N, D, ek, e = _fill(kind, P, N, D, ek, e, R[sel], TAP[sel], sp, rng, step)
        ge = sel[e]
        across = SG[ge] * 0.5
        vert = (P[:, 1] - C[ge, 1]) / (sp.strand_len + sp.frond_len + 0.1)
        parts.append(dict(p=P, n=N, d=D, ek=ek, pad=np.stack([across, vert, U[ge]], 1), bid=B[ge], eid=eids[ge],
                          kind=np.full(len(P), kind)))
    eid0 = NE
    for j, (bi, P, tg, rad, su) in enumerate(spur):
        ns = len(P)
        Nn = _nrm(np.tile(np.array([0, 0.85, 0]) + 0.5 * rad, (ns, 1)))
        parts.append(dict(p=P, n=Nn, d=np.tile(tg, (ns, 1)), ek=(su - 0.65) / 0.35,
                          pad=np.stack([np.zeros(ns), np.full(ns, 0.2), su], 1), bid=np.full(ns, bi),
                          eid=np.full(ns, eid0 + j), kind=np.full(ns, 3)))
    return {k: np.concatenate([q[k] for q in parts]) for k in ("p", "n", "d", "pad", "bid", "eid", "ek", "kind")}


def _fronds(C, T, S, R, TAP, sp, rng, step):
    ne = len(C)
    ln = sp.frond_len * TAP * rng.uniform(0.7, 1.25, ne)
    fwd = sp.frond_fwd * rng.uniform(0.7, 1.2, ne)
    dirv = S * np.cos(fwd)[:, None] + T * np.sin(fwd)[:, None]
    dr = sp.frond_droop * rng.uniform(0.6, 1.3, ne)
    n = np.maximum(2, (ln / step).astype(int) + 1)
    e, k = _ragged(n)
    t = k / (n[e] - 1)
    P = C[e] + (t * ln[e])[:, None] * dirv[e] - (dr[e] * ln[e] * t ** 2)[:, None] * UP
    D = _nrm(ln[e][:, None] * dirv[e] - (2 * dr[e] * ln[e] * t)[:, None] * UP)
    Ps, Ds, eks, es = [P], [D], [t], [e]
    pl = sp.pinna * ln
    has = pl > step * 1.2
    if has.any():
        idx = np.flatnonzero(has)
        npin = np.maximum(1, (ln[idx] / (pl[idx] * 0.9)).astype(int))
        pe, pk = _ragged(npin)                 # pinna records per element
        pe = idx[pe]
        tk = (pk + 0.5) / npin[np.searchsorted(idx, pe)]
        pe2 = np.repeat(pe, 2); tk2 = np.repeat(tk, 2); sg = np.tile([-1.0, 1.0], len(pe))
        base = C[pe2] + dirv[pe2] * (tk2 * ln[pe2])[:, None] - UP * (dr[pe2] * ln[pe2] * tk2 ** 2)[:, None]
        pdir = _nrm(dirv[pe2] * 0.5 + np.cross(dirv[pe2], UP) * sg[:, None])
        plen = pl[pe2] * (1 - 0.5 * tk2)
        m = np.maximum(1, (plen / step).astype(int))
        re, rk = _ragged(m)
        tt = (rk + 1) / m[re]
        Pp = base[re] + (tt * plen[re])[:, None] * pdir[re] - (tt * pl[pe2][re] * 0.5 * dr[pe2][re])[:, None] * UP
        Dp = pdir[re] - (0.4 * dr[pe2][re])[:, None] * UP
        Ps.append(Pp); Ds.append(Dp); eks.append(tk2[re]); es.append(pe2[re])
    P = np.concatenate(Ps); D = np.concatenate(Ds); ek = np.concatenate(eks); e = np.concatenate(es)
    nb = _nrm(UP + 0.35 * S + 0.25 * R)
    N = nb[e] + (ek ** 2 * dr[e])[:, None] * dirv[e] - (ek ** 2 * dr[e] * 0.6)[:, None] * UP
    return P, _nrm(N), D, ek, e


def _strands(C, T, S, R, TAP, sp, rng, step):
    ne = len(C); nf = sp.strand_fan
    ln = sp.strand_len * TAP * rng.uniform(0.6, 1.3, ne)
    fe = np.repeat(np.arange(ne), nf); ff = np.tile(np.arange(nf), ne)
    spread = (ff - (nf - 1) / 2) * 0.35
    dirv = _nrm(-UP + S[fe] * (0.25 + spread)[:, None] + T[fe] * 0.15 + R[fe] * 0.1)
    n = np.maximum(2, (ln[fe] / step).astype(int) + 1)
    re, rk = _ragged(n)
    t = rk / (n[re] - 1)
    e = fe[re]
    P = C[e] + (t * ln[e])[:, None] * dirv[re] + (t ** 2 * ln[e] * 0.15)[:, None] * R[e]
    N = _nrm(S * 0.75 + R * 0.25 + np.array([0, 0.35, 0]))[e]
    return P, N, dirv[re], t, e


def _brush(C, T, S, R, TAP, sp, rng, step):
    ne = len(C)
    ln = sp.brush_len * TAP * rng.uniform(0.7, 1.3, ne)
    k = rng.integers(3, 6, ne)
    te, _ = _ragged(k)
    a = rng.uniform(0, 2 * np.pi, len(te))
    dirv = _nrm(S[te] * (np.cos(a) * 0.8)[:, None] + UP * (np.sin(a) * 0.6)[:, None] + T[te] * 0.6 - UP * 0.35)
    n = np.maximum(1, (ln[te] / step).astype(int) + 1)
    re, rk = _ragged(n)
    t = np.where(n[re] > 1, rk / np.maximum(n[re] - 1, 1), 0.0)
    e = te[re]
    P = C[e] + S[e] * 0.03 + (t * ln[e])[:, None] * dirv[re] - (t ** 2 * ln[e] * 0.2)[:, None] * UP
    rel = P - C[e]
    N = _nrm(_nrm(rel) * 0.5 + np.array([0, 0.8, 0]) + 0.3 * R[e])
    return P, N, dirv[re], t, e


def _fill(kind, P, N, D, ek, e, R, TAP, sp, rng, step):
    # coarse scales: scatter points across the spray's leafy width so it doesn't read as sticks
    if kind == 0:
        hwe = sp.pinna * sp.frond_len * TAP * 0.8
        drawn = sp.pinna * sp.frond_len * TAP > step * 1.2
    elif kind == 1:
        hwe = 0.12 * sp.strand_len * TAP * sp.strand_fan + sp.needle
        drawn = np.zeros(len(TAP), bool)
    else:
        hwe = 0.5 * sp.brush_len * TAP
        drawn = np.zeros(len(TAP), bool)
    Rp = R[e]
    if step > 2 * sp.needle:
        want = (~drawn) & (hwe > 0.5 * step)
        nf = np.where(want, np.minimum(6, (1.6 * hwe / step).astype(int)) + 1, 0)[e]
        if nf.sum() > 0:
            fe, _ = _ragged(nf)
            hw = hwe[e][fe]
            base = N[fe] if kind == 0 else (Rp[fe] + 0.3 * UP)
            perp = _nrm(np.cross(D[fe], base))
            o = rng.uniform(-1, 1, len(fe)) * hw
            Pf = P[fe] + perp * o[:, None] - np.outer(np.abs(o) * 0.3, UP)
            P = np.concatenate([P, Pf]); N = np.concatenate([N, N[fe]]); D = np.concatenate([D, D[fe]])
            ek = np.concatenate([ek, ek[fe]]); e = np.concatenate([e, e[fe]]); Rp = np.concatenate([Rp, Rp[fe]])
    if sp.needle >= step * 0.9:
        k = int(sp.needle / step)
        base = N if kind == 0 else (Rp + 0.3 * UP)
        perp = _nrm(np.cross(D, base))
        outs = [P]; ns = [N]; ds = [D]; es = [ek]; ee = [e]
        for j in range(1, k + 1):
            for sg in (-1, 1):
                off = perp * (sg * j * step) - UP * (0.35 * j * step) + D * (0.4 * j * step)
                outs.append(P + off); ns.append(N); ds.append(D * 0.5 + perp * sg * 0.8 - UP * 0.3); es.append(ek); ee.append(e)
        P = np.concatenate(outs); N = np.concatenate(ns); D = np.concatenate(ds); ek = np.concatenate(es); e = np.concatenate(ee)
    return P, N, D, ek, e

def ground_ramp(hour, kind="floor", depth=0.0, fog=0.0):
    """6 steps: 0-2 shadow family, 3-5 lit. floor = needle litter + moss; meadow = grass."""
    h = HOURS[hour]
    A = _lin({"floor": (92, 84, 50), "meadow": (110, 120, 50), "rock": (120, 118, 112)}[kind])
    S = _lin(h["sun"]); K = _lin(h["sky"])
    out = [A * K * a for a in (0.06, 0.13, 0.22)]
    if h["ks"] > 0:
        out += [A * (S * b * h["ks"] * 1.6 + K * 0.25) for b in (0.35, 0.55, 0.8)]
    else:
        out += [A * K * b for b in (0.3, 0.38, 0.46)]
    return _srgb(_aerial(np.array(out), hour, depth, fog))

def _ground(out, dep, objs, cam, L, origin, mpp, hour, kind, fog, P):
    """Ground plane (y=0) under the stand, with the trees' cast shadows. Painted as a two-family
    field: the shadow is the floor in its shadow family (never a flat grey), the edge broken by
    the same noise that textures the floor."""
    H, W = out.shape[:2]
    ce, se = np.cos(cam.el), np.sin(cam.el)
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W].astype(float)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(P.get("seed", 0) + 3)
    if se < 0.05:     # side view: a ground band below the tree bases, 3 px
        m = yy >= origin[1] - 1
        gx = (xx - origin[0]) * mpp; gz = np.zeros_like(xx)
    else:
        gz = (yy - origin[1]) * mpp / se          # screen row -> ground depth (z)
        gx = (xx - origin[0]) * mpp
        m = np.ones((H, W), bool)
    # shadow mask: foliage and bole points dropped along the sun onto y = 0
    sh = np.zeros((H, W))
    Ly = max(L[1], 0.05)
    for d, t in objs:
        p = t.fol["p"]
        if len(p) > 40000:
            p = p[rng.choice(len(p), 40000, replace=False)]
        g = p - np.outer(p[:, 1] / Ly, L)
        g[:, 1] = 0
        sx, sy, _ = cam.project(g)
        ix = np.floor(sx + origin[0] + 0.5).astype(int); iy = np.floor(sy + origin[1] + 0.5).astype(int)
        ok = (ix >= 0) & (ix < W) & (iy >= 0) & (iy < H)
        np.add.at(sh, (iy[ok], ix[ok]), 1)
    from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
    sh = gaussian_filter(sh, 0.8)
    noise = rng.random((H, W))
    lit = (sh < 0.35 + 0.4 * (noise - 0.5)) 
    base = gaussian_filter(rng.random((H, W)), 1.2)
    base = (base - base.min()) / (np.ptp(base) + 1e-9)
    step = np.where(lit, 3 + np.floor(base * 2.2 + noise * 0.9).clip(0, 2), np.floor(base * 2.2 + noise * 0.9).clip(0, 2)).astype(int)
    depth = np.clip(-gz / 2000.0, 0, 0.9)
    for band in np.unique(np.round(depth * 10)):
        sel = m & (np.round(depth * 10) == band)
        ramp = palette.ground_ramp(hour, kind, band / 10, fog)
        out[sel] = ramp[step[sel]]
    gdep = np.where(m, -gz * ce - 1e-3 + 0 * se, -1e9)     # depth of the ground point toward viewer
    gdep = np.where(m, gz * ce * 0 + (0 * ce + gz * se) * 0 + gz * ce, -1e9)
    return out, np.maximum(dep, np.where(m, gz * cam.el * 0 + gz * ce, -1e9))
'''
open(p,'w').write(s)

"""Stage 5: the bole close up (scene zoom): Douglas-fir furrows, cedar buttress/flutes/strips,
hemlock scales, Sitka plates. Lower 8 m of an old tree at 0.03 m/px vs photos."""
import numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
import grow, paint, palette, cmp
PH={'douglas':'refs/lone_Douglas_fir_tree/openverse_Now_that_s_a_tree_1238d58e.jpg',
    'hemlock':'refs/western_hemlock_tree_drooping_leader/openverse_Dead_Western_Hemlock_Tree_with_Fungi_86aa6c7b.jpg',
    'cedar':'refs/old_growth_western_red_cedar_tree/openverse_Western_red_cedar_at_Willapa_National_Wild_f0b5f6dc.jpg',
    'spruce':'refs/Sitka_spruce_tree_Olympic/openverse_Tree_Hugger_8ef6ad8e.jpg'}
import glob
def ph(sp,H):
    f=PH[sp]
    if not glob.glob(f): f=glob.glob(f.split('_')[0]+'*')[0]
    g=glob.glob(f[:-12]+'*') or [f]
    im=Image.open(g[0]).convert('RGB'); w=int(im.width*H/im.height); return np.asarray(im.resize((w,H),Image.LANCZOS))
def bole(sp,mpp=0.03,W=200,H=270,hour='morning',sun=(20,30)):
    tr=grow.grow(sp,'old',seed=1,mpp=mpp)
    cam_y=0
    P=dict(seed=1,lit_frac=0.42)
    G,st=paint.paint_tree(tr,mpp,W,H,(W/2,H-6),sun=sun,P=P)
    bg=palette.sky(hour,H,W)
    pal=palette.palette(sp,hour)
    img=paint.compose(st,G['mat'],pal,bg)
    gr=palette.ground_ramp(hour)
    img[H-6:]=gr[1]
    return img
if __name__=='__main__':
    tag=sys.argv[1]; rows=[]
    for sp in ['douglas','hemlock','cedar','spruce']:
        try: p=ph(sp,270)
        except Exception as e: p=np.zeros((270,150,3),np.uint8); print(e)
        rows.append(cmp.panel([p,bole(sp)],[f'{sp} photo','old bole 0.03 m/px'],k=2))
    cmp.stack(rows).save(f'stage5/{tag}.png')

# replace bark_pattern

new_bp='''def _voronoi(x, y, seed):
    """F1, F2 and nearest-cell id of a jittered grid (unit cells), vectorized."""
    ix = np.floor(x).astype(np.int64); iy = np.floor(y).astype(np.int64)
    f1 = np.full(x.shape, 9.0); f2 = np.full(x.shape, 9.0); cid = np.zeros(x.shape, np.int64)
    dxn = np.zeros(x.shape); dyn = np.zeros(x.shape)
    for ox in (-1, 0, 1):
        for oy in (-1, 0, 1):
            cx = ix + ox; cy = iy + oy
            h = (cx * 73856093) ^ (cy * 19349663) ^ (seed * 83492791)
            jx = ((h * 2654435761) % 1000003) / 1000003.0
            jy = ((h * 40503 + 7) % 999983) / 999983.0
            px = cx + 0.15 + 0.7 * jx; py = cy + 0.15 + 0.7 * jy
            d = np.hypot(x - px, y - py)
            closer = d < f1
            f2 = np.where(closer, f1, np.minimum(f2, d))
            dxn = np.where(closer, x - px, dxn); dyn = np.where(closer, y - py, dyn)
            cid = np.where(closer, h, cid)
            f1 = np.where(closer, d, f1)
    return f1, f2, cid, dxn, dyn


BARK = {
    # cell size across (m), along the bole (m), crack width (fraction), crack depth steps, lip light
    "furrow":  dict(sx=0.16, sy=0.55, crack=0.34, deep=2, lip=1, wander=0.8),   # Douglas-fir: deep furrows, corky ridges
    "fibrous": dict(sx=0.045, sy=1.6, crack=0.22, deep=1, lip=0, wander=0.5),   # cedar: long fibrous strips
    "scaly":   dict(sx=0.08, sy=0.18, crack=0.25, deep=1, lip=1, wander=0.4),   # hemlock: scaly, shallow furrows
    "plates":  dict(sx=0.07, sy=0.06, crack=0.28, deep=1, lip=1, wander=0.2),   # Sitka: loose round scales
}


def bark_pattern(kind, u, v, mpp, seed, H):
    """Step offsets for bark, from a stretched Voronoi field in metres on the bole surface
    (u = arc length round the bole, v = height). Cracks between cells are the furrows; each
    plate/ridge catches a 1-px light lip on its upper edge. Features under ~2 px fade out (LOD)."""
    b = BARK.get(kind)
    if b is None:
        return 0
    x = u / b["sx"] + b["wander"] * np.sin(v / b["sy"] * 0.9 + 1.7 * np.sin(v * 0.37))
    y = v / b["sy"]
    f1, f2, cid, dx, dy = _voronoi(x, y, seed)
    edge = f2 - f1
    fade = np.clip((b["sx"] / mpp - 1.5) / 2.0, 0, 1)            # cell width in px
    out = np.where(edge < b["crack"] * 0.5, -b["deep"], np.where(edge < b["crack"], -1, 0))
    if b["lip"]:
        out = out + np.where((dy < -0.15) & (edge > b["crack"]) & (edge < b["crack"] * 2.2), 1, 0)
    # per-cell tone: some plates a step lighter or darker (weathered / fresh)
    tone = ((cid * 2246822519) % 7 == 0).astype(int) - ((cid * 3266489917) % 9 == 0).astype(int)
    out = out + tone
    return np.round(out * fade).astype(int)


def silver_mask(u, v, seed):
    """Old cedar bark weathers silver in long patches."""
    f1, f2, cid, dx, dy = _voronoi(u / 0.5, v / 4.0, seed + 11)
    return (cid * 2654435761) % 3 == 0


'''

Now the sheets module.

Write: elements/conifers/sheets.py
"""Labelled parameter sheets.  python3 sheets.py [A B C D E F]

A  species x age                       (each at a fitting scale, morning)
B  species x hour                      (mature, landscape scale; the palette is the light)
C  species x distance                  (scene crop, landscape, region, iso patch)
D  species x seed                      (mature individuals)
E  stands: mixed forest, side x hour; iso region
F  zoom strips: one tree per species through 0.05 .. 4 m/px at native pixels
"""
import numpy as np, sys, time, os
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
import conifer, grow, palette, cmp

SPECIES = ["douglas", "hemlock", "cedar", "spruce"]
AGES = ["sapling", "young", "mature", "old"]
HOURS = ["dawn", "morning", "noon", "golden", "dusk", "overcast", "fog", "moon"]
OUT = "sheets"
os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)


def label_grid(cells, rows, cols, k=2, title=None, gap=4, pad=70):
    """cells[r][c] images (uint8), all same size; row labels at left, col labels on top."""
    h, w = cells[0][0].shape[:2]
    Wt = pad + len(cols) * (w * k + gap); Ht = 30 + len(rows) * (h * k + gap)
    out = np.full((Ht, Wt, 3), 248, np.uint8)
    for r in range(len(rows)):
        for c in range(len(cols)):
            im = np.repeat(np.repeat(cells[r][c], k, 0), k, 1)
            y = 30 + r * (h * k + gap); x = pad + c * (w * k + gap)
            out[y:y + h * k, x:x + w * k] = im
    img = Image.fromarray(out); d = ImageDraw.Draw(img)
    if title:
        d.text((4, 2), title, fill=(0, 0, 0))
    for c, cl in enumerate(cols):
        d.text((pad + c * (w * k + gap) + 2, 16), str(cl), fill=(0, 0, 0))
    for r, rl in enumerate(rows):
        d.text((4, 30 + r * (h * k + gap) + 4), str(rl), fill=(0, 0, 0))
    return img


def fit_tree(sp, age, seed, W, H, hour="morning", margin=0.93, el=0.0, **kw):
    Ht = grow.grow(sp, age, seed=seed, mpp=1.0).H
    mpp = (Ht + 1.0) / (H * margin)
    rgb, info = conifer.tree(sp, age, seed=seed, mpp=mpp, W=W, H=H, hour=hour, el=el, **kw)
    return rgb, mpp


def sheet_A():
    W, H = 150, 250
    cells = []
    for sp in SPECIES:
        row = []
        for age in AGES:
            rgb, mpp = fit_tree(sp, age, 1, W, H)
            d = ImageDraw.Draw(im := Image.fromarray(rgb)); d.text((2, H - 12), f"{mpp:.2f} m/px", fill=(255, 255, 255))
            row.append(np.asarray(im))
        cells.append(row)
    label_grid(cells, SPECIES, AGES, k=2, title="A: species x age (each fitted to the frame; morning, sun from the left)").save(f"{OUT}/A_species_x_age.png")


def sheet_B():
    W, H = 110, 190
    cells = []
    for sp in SPECIES:
        row = []
        for hr in HOURS:
            rgb, mpp = fit_tree(sp, "mature", 2, W, H, hour=hr)
            row.append(rgb)
        cells.append(row)
    label_grid(cells, SPECIES, HOURS, k=2, title="B: species x hour (mature, ~0.25 m/px): the palette is the light").save(f"{OUT}/B_species_x_hour.png")


def sheet_D():
    W, H = 110, 200
    seeds = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]
    cells = [[fit_tree(sp, "mature", s, W, H)[0] for s in seeds] for sp in SPECIES]
    label_grid(cells, SPECIES, [f"seed {s}" for s in seeds], k=2, title="D: species x seed (mature individuals, fitted)").save(f"{OUT}/D_species_x_seed.png")
    cells = [[fit_tree(sp, "old", s, W, H)[0] for s in seeds] for sp in SPECIES]
    label_grid(cells, SPECIES, [f"seed {s}" for s in seeds], k=2, title="D2: species x seed (old growth, fitted)").save(f"{OUT}/D2_old_x_seed.png")


def patch(sp, n, w, d, seed, ages=("mature", "old", "young"), mix=None):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed); T = []
    for i in range(n):
        s = sp if mix is None else mix[rng.integers(len(mix))]
        T.append(dict(species=s, age=ages[rng.integers(len(ages))], seed=int(rng.integers(0, 5)),
                      x=rng.uniform(-w / 2, w / 2), z=-rng.uniform(0, d), yaw=rng.uniform(0, 6.28)))
    return T


def sheet_C():
    """per species: scene (upper crown at 0.04 m/px), landscape (0.25), region (1.5, a group),
    iso (30 deg, 1.0 m/px patch)."""
    W, H = 160, 200
    cols = ["scene 0.04 m/px (crown top)", "landscape 0.25", "region 1.5 (group)", "iso 30deg 1.0 (patch)", "region iso 2.5"]
    cells = []
    for sp in SPECIES:
        row = []
        tr = grow.grow(sp, "mature", seed=1, mpp=0.04)
        rgb, _ = conifer.tree(sp, "mature", seed=1, mpp=0.04, W=W, H=H, origin=(W / 2, H + (tr.H - 8) / 0.04), tr=tr)
        row.append(rgb)
        rgb, _ = fit_tree(sp, "mature", 1, W, H, margin=0.93)
        row.append(rgb)
        T = patch(sp, 14, 90, 60, 5)
        rgb, _ = conifer.stand(T, 1.5, W, H, origin=(W / 2, H - 4), ground="floor")
        row.append(rgb)
        T = patch(sp, 70, 170, 260, 7)
        rgb, _ = conifer.stand(T, 1.0, W, H, el=30, origin=(W / 2, H * 1.02), ground="floor")
        row.append(rgb)
        T = patch(sp, 160, 420, 650, 9)
        rgb, _ = conifer.stand(T, 2.5, W, H, el=30, origin=(W / 2, H * 1.02), ground="floor")
        row.append(rgb)
        cells.append(row)
        print("C", sp, flush=True)
    label_grid(cells, SPECIES, cols, k=2, title="C: species x distance/zoom (morning)").save(f"{OUT}/C_species_x_distance.png")


def sheet_E():
    W, H = 300, 170
    T = patch(None, 34, 110, 70, 11, mix=SPECIES)
    cells = []
    hrs = ["morning", "golden", "overcast", "dusk"]
    for hr in hrs:
        rgb, _ = conifer.stand(T, 0.35, W, H, hour=hr, origin=(W / 2, H - 4), ground="floor")
        cells.append([rgb])
        print("E", hr, flush=True)
    label_grid(cells, hrs, ["mixed stand, side view, 0.35 m/px"], k=2, title="E: a mixed stand at four hours").save(f"{OUT}/E_stand_x_hour.png")
    W, H = 320, 220
    T = patch(None, 700, 360, 500, 13, mix=SPECIES)
    rgb, _ = conifer.stand(T, 1.2, W, H, el=30, origin=(W / 2, H * 1.02), ground="floor")
    label_grid([[rgb]], ["iso"], ["mixed old forest, iso 30deg, 1.2 m/px, morning"], k=2).save(f"{OUT}/E2_iso_forest.png")


def sheet_F():
    MPPS = [0.05, 0.08, 0.13, 0.2, 0.32, 0.5, 0.8, 1.25, 2.0, 3.2]
    rows = []
    for sp in SPECIES:
        Ht = grow.grow(sp, "mature", seed=2, mpp=1.0).H
        cells, labs = [], []
        for m in MPPS:
            Hp = int(Ht * 1.05 / m) + 4; Wp = max(10, int(Hp * 0.45))
            rgb, _ = conifer.tree(sp, "mature", seed=2, mpp=m, W=Wp, H=Hp)
            cells.append(rgb); labs.append(f"{m}")
        Hm = max(c.shape[0] for c in cells)
        padded = []
        for c in cells:
            p = np.full((Hm, c.shape[1], 3), 248, np.uint8); p[Hm - c.shape[0]:] = c; padded.append(p)
        rows.append(cmp.panel(padded, [f"{sp} {l} m/px" if i == 0 else l for i, l in enumerate(labs)], k=1, gap=3))
        print("F", sp, flush=True)
    cmp.stack(rows).save(f"{OUT}/F_zoom_strips_1x.png")

# Critique (newest first)

From the studio critic (second round of critics). These are my reads, not gates: you own the work and decide. Viewing crops are in `elements/_critic2/rN/`.

## Round 3 — 2026-09-26 00:44 — `stage4/st2–st4.png` (stands), `stage5/b1–b3.png` (old boles)
(My read; your call.)

**st4 (500 trees, 0.8 m/px, 30° down) reads as a PNW forest at 1×.** Upright spires, dark gaps between crowns, haze deepening up-slope. The composition is right.

What gives it away at 8× (`_critic2/r3/con_st4.png`):

1. **Every crown is salt and pepper.** Sage, yellow and navy single pixels scattered evenly through each spire, with no lit cap and no shadow side. The aerial study's measured rule (READY.md, "Far and iso") is a lit cap on the sun side of each crown, lit share 0.35–0.56, dark on the other side. Your cedar in st1 already does tier-top light; at this distance it should collapse to one lit flank per spire plus a few tier-edge ticks.
2. **Yellow-lit crowns are random per tree.** Some spires are gold, their neighbours sage, independent of position or sun side. It reads as a species mix or autumn larch. If it's meant as sunlit vs shaded trees, tie it to the terrain and the sun.

The boles (`b3`):

- **Cedar is the strong one**: flared, fluted buttress with the flutes carried in light and dark bands. It reads beside the photo.
- **Spruce is sandpaper**: single-pixel tan/navy noise over the whole bole. Sitka bark is thin, flaking scales, and the photo's bole is mossy, with buttress roots. Scales want small 2–3 px plates with a dark lower lip, not pixel noise.
- **Douglas-fir**: the photo's bark is deep furrows between thick corky ridges broken into long blocks. Yours has the right vertical grain but the ridges don't catch light; give each ridge a lit edge on the sun side and a dark furrow beside it, at a coarser spacing.
- All four have a hard vertical terminator and a near-black shadow half. Old boles in the forest get strong reflected light from the floor and neighbouring trunks; lift the shadow side one or two steps and let the bark texture show in it.


## Round 2 — 2026-09-26 00:22 — `stage4/st1.png` (24-tree stand, morning, 0.3 m/px)
**The front cedar is a real step: light now rides the tier tops.** (`_critic2/r2/con_st1_cedar.png`.) Each upturned spray catches olive-gold on its upper edge on *both* sides of the trunk, with the pad below in dark green-black. That's the V16 mechanism from round 1, and at 8× this crown is the closest thing to Ferrari in the folder. The haze ladder (warm near, blue-grey mid, pale ghost far) also gives the stand real depth at 1×.

What I see now:

1. **The mid-layer trees are salt and pepper** (`con_st1_mid.png`, the tree at x≈480–620, y≈120–300). Their crowns are an even scatter of pale sage, blue-grey and navy single pixels, with no tiers and no lit tier-tops. The fix you made to the cedar hasn't propagated down the haze ladder. With distance the tier structure should simplify (fewer tiers, each a flat pad with

    y = v / b["sy"]

    if kind in ("furrow", "fibrous"):
        # long ridges: wandering vertical stripes, furrow on each stripe's right, lit rim on its
        # left (sun side), broken into long blocks by short horizontal checks
        k = np.floor(x).astype(np.int64); f = x - k
        hk = ((k * 2654435761 + seed * 97) % 1000003) / 1000003.0
        blk = y * (0.7 + 0.6 * hk) + hk * 5
        fb = blk - np.floor(blk)
        out = np.where(f > 1 - b["crack"], -b["deep"], 0)
        out = np.where((f > 1 - b["crack"] * 1.6) & (out == 0), -1, out)
        out = out + np.where(f < 0.18, 1, 0) * b["lip"]
        out = out + np.where((fb < 0.06) & (f < 1 - b["crack"]), -1, 0)
        tone = ((np.floor(blk).astype(np.int64) * 31 + k * 17) % 7 == 0).astype(int)
        return np.round((out + tone) * fade).astype(int)
    f1, f2, cid, dx, dy = _voronoi(x, y, seed)'''
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new)

    # thin lights (V16): bright steps live on the top edges of tiers -- a 1-2 px run where the pixel

# The great Pacific Northwest conifers, after Mark Ferrari: lessons

A student's log. Subject: Douglas-fir, western hemlock, western redcedar and Sitka spruce, as
individuals and as forest, from 1-cm needles to a 2.5 m/px iso region.

**Code** (all in this folder; numpy only, `nice -n 10`, one BLAS thread):

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `grow.py` | the four species grown as geometry in metres: trunk (lean, taper, buttress flare, flutes, hemlock's nodding leader), limbs in whorls or scattered with species droop / J-hook upturn, secondary *fans* on mature and old limbs, old-crown gaps, dead stubs, cedar candelabra leaders and silver spike tops. Species and age tables (`SP`, `AGE`) are the parameters. |
| `foliage_fast.py` | the foliage *elements* (fronds, strands, brush tufts, spurs, needle fill, coarse-scale area fill), vectorized (10-25x faster than the loop version `grow._foliage_slow`, which it matches; `tmp/vectorize_check.png`). |
| `paint.py` | the painter: light per foliage point, rasterize body + stroke marks, value plan, quantize to an index canvas (material, step), wood surfaces with bark patterns. |
| `palette.py` | the palette is the light: 8-step foliage ramps (shadow family 0-3, lit family 4-7) per species x hour x aerial depth; wood, ground and sky ramps. |
| `conifer.py` | public API: `tree(...)`, `stand(...)` (depth-composited stands with one shared shadow map and a ground plane with cast shadows). |
| `sheets.py` | the labelled parameter sheets in `sheets/`. |
| `tree.py`, `render.py`, `s0_copy.py`, `s0_tune.py`, `stats.py`, `lw.py` | stage 0 (the master copy), frozen as its regression. |
| `s1_*.py` ... `s5_bark.py` | one script per study stage; `cmp.py` side-by-side helpers; `cmp_voxtrees.py`. |

Photos: `refs/` (fetched with the studio's imgfetch; each folder has a `_sheet.jpg` contact
sheet). The ones I leaned on: the Hoh hemlock against sky
(`Tsuga_heterophylla/commons_Hoh_Rainforest_...`), the open-grown Sitka spruce
(`Douglas_fir_tree_crown/openverse_Sitka_Spruce_b36ebb43.jpg`), Big Lonely Doug
(`lone_Douglas_fir_tree/openverse_Big_Lonely_Doug_97f10fa9.jpg`), the ancient cedar
(`western_redcedar_candelabra_top/openverse_Thuja_plicata_..._ancient_form_...`), the Willapa cedar
bole, and the naturalist desk's guides (`~/work/naturalist/plants/02-trees.md`, `05-old-growth.md`),
which gave me real heights (mature 40-60 m, old 60-75 m), the bark and foliage colours, and the
single most useful sentence for a painter: *every hemlock's tip nods over like a bent fishing rod.*

---

## Stage 0: copy Ferrari first (V16, the alpine tarn)

Ferrari's only conifer passage in *Living Worlds* is V16 (and its PM pair): subalpine spires round a
tarn, at three distances. I copied C1, the mid-distance spire against sky, native (420,30)-(480,130),
with a *procedural* tree (not a traced index map), so what I learned would transfer. The copy uses his
palette; everything at pixel level is generated. Progression `stage0/c1_v1.png` ... `c1_v10.png`.

![first](stage0/c1_v1.png)
![last](stage0/c1_v10.png)

What reading his indices showed (`stats.py`, classes over the tree's pixels):

- **The conifer is a dark mass with light on it**, not a green shape shaded: black 47%, dark olive 23%,
  bright olive 27%, dark green 3%. Morning light makes the lit family *olive/ochre*, never leaf green:
  (143,151,35) and (151,163,63) over (79,83,19) and a brown (95,71,0); the shadow family is black
  through (0,31,15).
- **Mean run length is ~1.5 px in every class.** At mid distance the texture is a fine intermix of three
  very different values. My first versions had lit runs of 3-4 px (solid olive "pads") and read as
  cut paper. The fix was a *stochastic three-way mixture* per pixel (black / dark olive / lit) whose
  proportions follow the light, instead of a threshold dither. With it the class shares and run
  lengths match to within 0.02 and 0.3 px.
- **Spurs make the silhouette**: the needled branch tip runs out horizontally past the pad, every
  2-4 rows, alternating sides, longer lower down. Without an explicit spur line the edge was a round
  cloud.
- **The spire is 1 px** for ~5 rows with lit/dark alternating, then crosses.

Honest state: at 1x and 2x (`stage0/c1_v10_2x.png`) the copy passes; at 8x his tiers are more
regular drooping shelves and his lit area is more continuous across the crown than mine. I stopped
here because the goal was the grammar, not his tree.

---

## Stage 1: the smallest form, then one tree

### 1a. One limb (`s1_branch.py`, `stage1/b1.png` -> `b3.png`)

![b1](stage1/b1.png)

The first limb render was the most useful failure of the study: at 2 cm/px my sprays were **stick
drawings**, 1-px skeletons of rachis and pinnae, and all black. Two lessons:

- A spray at near scale is an *area* (needles have width): each element now fills its leafy half-width
  (`needle`, 1.5-3 cm per species) when that width is over a pixel.
- At coarse scale the opposite: the spray's area is under a pixel or two, and the line skeleton read as
  sticks again at 0.2-0.3 m/px (old trees looked like flags on poles, `stage2/a1.png`). The fix is a
  coarse-scale *area fill*: scatter points across the spray's width (`_fill`).
The elements, per species (the naturalist's descriptions made concrete):

- hemlock: flat lacy **fronds** off both sides of each twig, arcing down at their tips, a few short
  pendant tips; slender drooping limbs; the nodding leader.
- cedar: **J-hook limbs** (droop then sweep up), carrying **hanging fans** (3-strand curtains) with flat
  fronds at the upturned tips.
- Sitka spruce: stiff short fronds on top of long horizontal limbs that upturn at the tips, with
  **pendulous branchlets** hanging beneath (the curtains of the open-grown photo).
- Douglas-fir: **brush tufts** (needles all round the twig, "bottle-brush") plus flatter fronds.
Every limb ends in a **spur** (stage 0), and spray length falls off toward the tip (`wedge`) so tiers
end in points, not blobs.

### 1b. One tree (`s1_col.py`, `s1_near.py`, `stage1/n1` -> `n6`, `relight*`)

![n6](stage1/n6.png)

- **Crown fullness came from element size, not branch count.** Early crowns were sparse skeletons;
  scaling element size by age (`AGE[..]["el"]`) and adding fans filled them.
- **The value plan must be decided** (`lit_frac`): the lit family's share of the foliage is a
  quantile of the direct light, not whatever the renderer produced. 0.42 for side light.
- **Element coherence**: at near scales each spray takes one mixture draw, so sprays read as marks
  instead of per-pixel static.

**The critic's round-1 note changed the lighting, and it was right**: my trees were lit as two flat
halves (a vertical terminator down the trunk), because I weighted the crown's cone normal at 0.5.
Ferrari's are a dark mass lit along **the tops of the tiers, on both sides**, strongest toward the
sun. With the crown normal down to 0.18 the sprays' own upward normals take over and light rides the
tier tops (`stage1/relight.png`). Then (round 4): **thin lights** — bright steps only on the top
1-2 px of each tier (the pixel above is sky, another branch, or shadow), the tier's inside dropping to
dark olive; the shadow family keeps two dark greens of texture instead of flat black; and at near
scale the grain goes **vertical** (SYNTHESIS: grain grows with nearness): the per-pixel draw becomes
1-px columns of correlated noise, and short olive strands hang from lit tier tops.

![relight5](stage1/relight5_zoom.png)

Beside V16 at 5x (`stage1/relight5_zoom.png`, the "coh0.3 vg2.5" column is the adopted setting): the
tier structure, dark mass and thin lit tops are now his. **Not there yet**: his lights are warmer and
higher in contrast, and his vertical strands are crisper (whole lit columns 3-5 px with black
between); mine are still partly mottle.

---

## Stage 2: species x age (`s2_ages.py`, `stage2/a1` -> `a3`; final: `sheets/A_species_x_age.png`)

![a3](stage2/a3.png)

The naturalist guide moved the forms more than anything I did alone: real heights, "the crown occupies
the top third to half" for mature Douglas-fir, "often only the top 10 to 20 m of a 60 to 70 m tree"
for old ones, the flat broken tops, cedar's multiple leaders and silver spikes, spruce's long
horizontal limbs. What the ages carry:

- sapling: a full cone to the ground, dense short sprays;
- young: cone to near the ground, upswept tips high, drooping low (hemlock and cedar keep crowns low:
  shade-tolerant);
- mature: long clear bole with dead stubs; crown 40-60% of the height; fans on the limbs; gaps;
- old: 65-75 m, huge girth and buttress, crown in the top ~30% with big gaps and heavy limbs,
  flattened/rounded top; cedar candelabra (2-4 reiterated leaders, some dead) and dead spike tops
  (drawn silver, never thinner than ~1.5 px so they read).

The critic's first read: "species read at a glance... hemlock mature/old (drooping tiers, the leaning
top) and cedar old (flared, fluted red base, upturned candelabra tips) are recognisably those
trees." **Weak:** saplings are straggly at their scale; young Douglas-fir read as a cypress cloud until I
cut the brush tufts and added flat fronds; old hemlock droops too much toward willow (tamed:
droop 0.45 -> 0.32).

---

## Stage 3: every distance (`s3_zoom.py`, `sheets/F_zoom_strips_1x.png`)

![z2](stage3/z2_1x.png)

One tree swept from 0.05 to 4 m/px at native pixels. What makes the ladder hold without pops:

- geometry is the same tree at every scale; only the *sampling* changes (point spacing 0.45 px; element
  anchor spacing >= 0.5 px; fill switches from needles to area at `step > 2 x needle`);
- the mark handoff is continuous: stroke length = `mark_m / mpp` clipped to 1..4 px; element coherence
  rises and **branch coherence** takes over past ~0.1 m/px (the whole branch shares one draw, so its
  top edge is one lit run — the "one lit flank per spire plus a few tier-edge ticks" of the aerial
  study); the vertical grain fades out by 0.3 m/px;
- the value plan (lit share) is the same at every scale, so the tree doesn't brighten or darken as it
  shrinks. At 2-4 m/px a mature tree is still a spire with a lit flank and a trunk, not a speck.

Honest: at 1-2.5 m/px the silhouette is right but the lit pixels are few and scattered; Ferrari's far
trees (V16 row of small spires) have a crisper 1-px spire with alternating spur ticks.

---

## Stage 4: several together, then a forest (`s4_stand.py`, `conifer.stand`, sheets C and E)

![st2](stage4/st2.png)
![st4](stage4/st4.png)

- **One shared opacity shadow map** for every tree's foliage: trees shade each other, and the forest
  interior goes dark by itself. The lower, shaded trees in `st2` fall into the blue-green shadow family.
- **One value plan for the whole stand**: the lit threshold is a quantile over all trees together, so a
  tree in its neighbours' shade stays dark (a per-tree quantile lit every tree equally).
- **Aerial perspective** in the palette (mix toward the horizon; darks lift most, lights hold; a
  violet bias in far darks) keyed to metres. My first `far` of 300 m made 60 m look like 5 km;
  realistic is ~2000 m, and the ground uses 4000 m.
- **The ground** (`_ground`): for the iso view every screen row maps to a ground depth; cast shadows are
  the foliage dropped along the sun onto y=0, painted as the floor in its shadow family (never a flat
  grey), band seams dithered.
- **Density matters more than anything at iso**: at 16 trees/ha the stand reads as ponderosa parkland
  (critic, round 4); westside forest is closed canopy. The sheets now use ~70-90 trees/ha of mixed ages.

Honest: in iso, crowns are still grainy at 8x, and random species mixing gives a confetti of gold
cedars and blue spruces; real stands come in patches. That's a placement question (the simulator's).

---

## Stage 5: the bole close up (`s5_bark.py`, `stage5/b1` -> `b6`)

![b6](stage5/b6.png)

- **Cedar's fluted buttress works**: the flutes are a radius ripple near the ground *and* a normal
  perturbation (dR/dθ), so the valleys turn from the light and carry dark bands up into the flare. It
  reads beside the Willapa photo (critic: "the strong one").
- Bark is designed in metres on the bole surface and faded out when a feature drops under ~1.5 px:
  Douglas-fir as **long wandering ridges** (furrow on the right of each, lit rim on the sun side, long
  blocks broken by short checks); cedar as long fibrous strips; hemlock and spruce as small plates with a
  lit upper rim and a dark lower lip (a stretched Voronoi field).
- Hard material, so **ordered 2x2 dither**, and only a narrow seam; a strong dither turned every bole to
  sandpaper.
- Old boles get **bounce light** (floor and neighbouring trunks) so the shadow side keeps texture.

**Not there yet**: the Douglas-fir ridges are too regular (a corrugated pillar more than corky blocks);
spruce bark is still too fine to read at 0.03 m/px; no moss (the Hoh boles are furred with it), no
conks, no root flare over nurse logs.

---

## Stage 6: the light (`palette.py`, `sheets/B_species_x_hour.png`)

Eight light states: dawn, morning, noon, golden, dusk, overcast, fog, moon. The index canvas holds
material and step; the hour is a ramp swap plus the sun's elevation. Overcast and fog have no sun
family: the "lit" steps become the sky-lit tops at low contrast, which is the westside's usual light
(the naturalist: "soft, overcast and blue-grey... conifers read almost black-green"). Fog adds a
flat veil toward the sky colour.

---

## Beside the existing trees (`compare/voxtrees_vs_mine.png`)

![vt](compare/voxtrees_vs_mine.png)

vox-trees' spruce at its own sheet scale (0.42 m/px) is a small, clean, chunky sprite with a strong
ground shadow; it reads instantly. Mine at the same scale are thinner, lighter-edged spires; at that
size vox-trees reads better, honestly. Where this study goes past them: the four PNW species are
distinct (the nodding hemlock, cedar's J-limbs, fluted buttress and candelabra, spruce's long limbs
and curtains, the old Douglas-fir's pillar and ragged top), the ages are real (a 70 m old-growth tree
is a different silhouette, not a scaled young one), and at 0.05-0.25 m/px the tier-top light and
hanging-strand texture come from Ferrari's V16 grammar.

---

## The sheets (`sheets/`)

- `A_species_x_age.png` — species x age, each fitted to the frame (scale printed).
- `B_species_x_hour.png` — species x eight hours (mature).
- `C_species_x_distance.png` — species x (scene 0.04 crown top, landscape 0.25, region 1.5 group,
  iso 1.0 patch, iso 2.5 region).
- `D_species_x_seed.png`, `D2_old_x_seed.png` — individuals.
- `E_stand_x_hour.png`, `E2_iso_forest.png` — a mixed stand at four hours; a mixed old forest in iso.
- `F_zoom_strips_1x.png` — each species through 0.05 ... 3.2 m/px at native pixels.

## The technique as parameters

`conifer.tree(species, age, seed, mpp, hour, sun_az, el, depth, fog, P, openness, lean)`. Painter
parameters `P` (defaults in `conifer.DEFAULT_P`, scale handoffs in `conifer.scale_P`): `lit_frac`
(value plan), `wc` (crown-normal weight), `p_lit`/`p_dol` (mixture), `mark_frac`/`mark_m` (strokes),
`elem_coh`/`branch_coh` (coherence unit), `vgrain` (vertical grain), `strand_p` (hanging strands),
`edge_light`, `dark_tex`, `shadow_k`, `bole_sink`/`limb_sink`, `bounce`. Species and ages are the
tables `grow.SP` and `grow.AGE`.

## What would help

- A photo set of the four species **as whole trees against sky at 100-500 m** (a ridge line, a
  clearcut edge): most photos are trunk-up or needle-close, and the middle distance is where these
  trees are most seen.
- The simulator's stand structure (patches, gaps, snags, species by site) for the iso view: random
  mixing is the biggest thing wrong with my forest sheets.
- Moss and epiphytes for the Hoh variants (the naturalist's `Hoh_rainforest_moss_bigleaf_maple`).
